	+ It is a comprehensive theological treatise
* **Table of Contents**
	+ The book is divided into six parts
	+ The parts cover various topics in theology, including God, creation, the fall, redemption, and the last day
* **Brief Memoir of Thomas Watson**
	+ Watson was a prominent Puritan theologian
	+ He was educated at Emmanuel College, Cambridge
	+ He was a prolific writer and produced several valuable works
* **Characteristics of Watson's Writing**
	+ He was concise, racy, illustrative, and suggestive
	+ His writing combined sound doctrine, heart-searching experience, and practical wisdom
* **Usefulness of Watson's Works**
	+ His works are highly valued by students and ministers
	+ They are considered a treasure of Puritan literature

	+ Little is known about Watson's life, including his birth and death dates
	+ He was educated at Emmanuel College, Cambridge, which was known for producing Puritan theologians
* **Puritanism and Emmanuel College**
	+ Emmanuel College was a breeding ground for Puritan theologians
	+ Many prominent Puritan ministers were educated there

	+ Each topic is explored in depth and with biblical support

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Thomas Watson's Background**
	+ Studied at Cambridge, known for being a laborious student
	+ Became a successful preacher and rector of St Stephen's, Walbrook in London

• **Ministry at St Stephen's**
	+ Served as a faithful pastor for nearly 16 years
	+ Church was constantly filled, with Watson's fame and popularity growing
	+ He was known for his piety, usefulness, and gift of prayer

• **Respect and Admiration**
	+ Calamy described him as a man of considerable reputation, a popular and judicious preacher
	+ Bishop Richardson was impressed by his sermon and prayer, and asked for a copy of his prayer

• **Personal Qualities**
	+ Watson was a man of prayer, able to pray extempore with great effectiveness
	+ He was not a factious person, but rather a loyal and devout Christian

• **The Act of Uniformity**
	+ The Act forced many clergy to choose between their conscience and their livings
	+ Watson, as a Puritan, was not tolerated by the dominant spirits in the Establishment

• **Watson's Decision**
	+ He chose to submit to the loss of his living rather than compromise his conscience
	+ He was not a rebel or a hater of royalty, but rather a loyal Christian who prioritized his faith

• **Consequences of the Act**
	+ The Act led to the ejection of many clergy, including Watson
	+ This event marked the beginning of a real reformation in the Church of England

• **Progress of the Reformation**
	+ The cause of the ejected clergy gained strength and momentum
	+ The Establishment was pushed towards the brink of collapse

• **Watson's Departure**
	+ The congregation of St Stephen's was devastated by his departure
	+ Watson spoke with joy and sorrow, as he left behind his flock

• **Legacy of Watson**
	+ He was remembered as a faithful pastor, a popular and judicious preacher
	+ His legacy continued to inspire and influence the Church of England for generations to come

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Watson's Farewell Sermons**
	+ Watson preached three farewell sermons before being ejected from his parish
	+ The sermons were delivered on August 17th and 19th

• **First Sermon (August 17th)**
	+ Preached on John 13:34, "A new commandment I give unto you, that ye love one another"
	+ Emphasized the importance of loving enemies and persecutors

• **Second Sermon (August 17th)**
	+ Preached on 2 Corinthians 7:1, "Having therefore these promises, dearly beloved, let us cleanse ourselves from all filthiness of the flesh and spirit, perfecting holiness in the fear of God"
	+ Emphasized the importance of a right gospel minister's love for their people
	+ Watson expressed his love and gratitude for his congregation

• **Watson's Love for His Congregation**
	+ Watson had been ministering to his congregation for almost 16 years
	+ He expressed his joy and gratitude for their love and support
	+ He reassured them that he would continue to love and pray for them even if he was no longer able to preach to them

• **Charges Against Watson**
	+ Some accused Watson of being disloyal and seditious
	+ Watson denied these charges and emphasized his loyalty to the king

• **Watson's Final Words**
	+ Watson gave his congregation 20 admirable directions to live by
	+ He encouraged them to treasure these directions as jewels in their hearts
	+ He expressed his desire to be guided by God's word and providence

• **Third Sermon (August 19th)**
	+ Preached on Isaiah 3:10-11, "Say ye to the righteous, that it shall be well with him: for they shall eat the fruit of their doings. Woe unto the wicked! it shall be ill with him, for the reward of his hands shall be given him"
	+ Emphasized the importance of living a righteous life

• **Watson's Continued Ministry**
	+ After being ejected from his parish, Watson continued to preach occasionally whenever he could do so with safety
	+ He preached in secret assemblies, often in barns, kitchens, or other hidden locations

• **The Significance of Secret Assemblies**
	+ These secret assemblies were a source of comfort and joy for the faithful
	+ The word of God was precious in these times of persecution

• **The Legacy of Watson's Ministry**
	+ Watson's ministry had a lasting impact on his congregation
	+ His sermons and teachings continued to inspire and guide them even after he was gone

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Life and Ministry of Mr. Watson**
	+ Fitted up rooms for Nonconformists to attend
	+ Licensed the great hall in Crosby House in 1672
	+ Preached there for several years
* **Collaboration with Rev. Stephen Charnock**
	+ Became joint pastor with Charnock at Crosby Hall in 1675
	+ Charnock wrote a Body of Divinity, including the Divine Attributes
* **Death and Legacy**
	+ Returned to Essex and died suddenly in 1689 or 1690
	+ Published a Body of Divinity after his death
* **Influence on Others**
	+ Colonel James Gardiner was converted after reading Watson's book "The Christian Soldier"
	+ Gardiner had a profound spiritual experience after reading the book
* **Published Works**
	+ Published various books on practical subjects
	+ Principal work was a Body of Divinity in 176 sermons on the Assembly's Catechism
* **Publication of the Body of Divinity**
	+ Published in one volume folio in 1692
	+ Accompanied by a portrait of Watson and a recommendatory preface
* **Impact and Value**
	+ Continued to train common people in theology for many years
	+ Still commonly found in Scottish cottages
	+ Valuable resource for students and pastors
* **Editorial Note**
	+ Rev. George Rogers superintended the issue of the present edition
	+ Notes the unique value of Watson's work in providing sermon matter within a small compass
* **Comparison to Other Authors**
	+ Unlike Howe, Charnock, and Owen, Watson's work is concise and utilizable
	+ Would be of great value to students and pastors
* **Historical Significance**
	+ Watson's work was widely read and influential in its time
	+ Still relevant and useful today

• **Importance of Being Settled in Faith**
	+ It is the duty of Christians to be settled in the doctrine of faith
	+ The apostle's prayer is for Christians to be established, strengthened, and settled

• **Consequences of Being Unsettled**
	+ Being unsettled in true religion argues lack of judgment
	+ It leads to a lack of steadfastness and wandering from one opinion to another
	+ Unsettled Christians are compared to infants, tossed about by every wind of teaching

• **Characteristics of Unsettled Christians**
	+ They are fickle and childish, embracing and rejecting truths at whim
	+ They are easily influenced by every wind of doctrine
	+ They are prone to heresy and damnable teachings

• **The Need for Grounding in Faith**
	+ Christians need to be well grounded in the doctrine of faith
	+ This is the best way to be settled in true religion
	+ Grounding in faith prevents wandering and instability

• **The Importance of Judgment**
	+ Christians need to exercise judgment in their faith
	+ Lack of judgment leads to instability and wandering
	+ Judgment is necessary to discern between true and false teachings

• **The Role of the Church**

	+ The church should guide Christians in their understanding of true religion

• **The Danger of Heresy**
	+ Heresy is a serious threat to Christian faith
	+ Heresy can lead to damnable teachings and eternal consequences
	+ Christians need to be vigilant against heresy and false teachings

• **The Goal of Christian Education**
	+ The goal of Christian education is to establish and strengthen Christians in their faith
	+ This involves grounding Christians in the doctrine of faith
	+ Christian education should aim to produce stable and steadfast Christians.

	+ To bring people to a settlement in true religion

	+ Requires being settled in true religion
	+ Is impossible without a strong foundation in faith

	+ Use fine elegant phrases and flattering language to deceive the weak
	+ Pretend to be men of zeal and sanctity, and to be divinely inspired
	+ Pretend to have new revelations to gain admiration and followers

• **The danger of being deceived**
	+ Can lead to being as useless as a crooked bow
	+ Can make God angry and jealous
	+ Can lead to being dropped into the devil's mouth like a wind-fall

• **The importance of being settled**
	+ Is essential for growth in faith and godliness
	+ Is necessary for being a true Christian and honoring God
	+ Is a matter of great need, given the many things that can unsettle us.

	+ False teachers try to bring down sound orthodox teachers

• **The Importance of Grounding in Knowledge**

• **The Chief End of Man**
	+ Man's chief end is to glorify God and to enjoy him forever
	+ There are two ends of life: glorifying God and enjoying God

• **The Glorifying of God**
	+ The glory of God is a silver thread that must run through all our actions
	+ Everything we do should be done to the glory of God
	+ Glorifying God respects all three persons of the Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit

• **Understanding God's Glory**
	+ There is a twofold glory: intrinsic glory (God's glory in himself) and extrinsic glory (the glory that God receives from his creatures)
	+ God's glory is essential to his being, like light is to the sun

• **The Importance of Glorifying God**
	+ Glorifying God is the chief end of man
	+ We should prioritize glorifying God in all our actions and decisions

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Glory**
	+ God's life lies in his glory
	+ His glory is infinite and cannot be added to
	+ God will not part with his essential glory

• **What is Glorifying God?**
	+ Consists of four things: appreciation, adoration, affection, and subjection
	+ This is the "yearly rent we pay to the crown of heaven"

• **Appreciation**
	+ Setting God highest in our thoughts
	+ Having a venerable esteem of him
	+ Admiring God's attributes, promises, and works

• **Adoration (Worship)**
	+ A civil reverence for people of honor
	+ A divine worship given to God as his royal prerogative
	+ Must be according to the pattern prescribed in God's word

• **Types of Worship**
	+ Civil reverence: showing respect to people of honor
	+ Divine worship: showing respect to God as his royal prerogative

• **Importance of Divine Worship**
	+ God is very jealous of divine worship
	+ It is the apple of his eye, the pearl of his crown
	+ Must be done according to God's appointed pattern

• **Affection**
	+ Part of the glory we give to God
	+ Counts himself glorified when he is loved
	+ There are two types of love:
		- Love of concupiscence (self-love)
		- Love of delight (taking delight in God himself)

• **Love of Delight**
	+ A love that takes delight in God himself
	+ Not just a love of God's blessings or benefits

• **Subjection**
	+ Part of glorifying God
	+ Involves submitting to God's will and authority

• **Glorifying God in Practice**
	+ Involves setting God highest in our thoughts and actions
	+ Involves worshiping God in spirit and truth
	+ Involves loving God with all our heart, soul, and strength

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**1. What it means to love God**
	* To love God is to have an exuberant and superlative love for Him
	* It's a love that gives God the best of our love and affections
	* It's a love that's intense and ardent, like the love of a spouse

**2. Glorifying God**
	* Glorifying God means to dedicate ourselves to His service
	* It means to be devoted to His service and to stand ready to obey Him
	* It means to live a life of obedience and service to God

**3. Why we must glorify God**
	* Because He gives us our being and life
	* Because He gives us health, food, and all the comforts of life
	* Because we live by Him and should live to Him

**4. God's glory**
	* God has made all things for His own glory
	* He will have glory out of everything, even from the wicked
	* The godly are the lively organs of His praise and should show forth His glory

**5. The value of God's glory**
	* God's glory has intrinsic value and excellence
	* It transcends the thoughts of men and the tongues of angels
	* It's more worth than heaven and the salvation of all men's souls

**6. The importance of obedience**
	* A good Christian is like the sun, which sends forth heat and goes its circuit round the world
	* We must not only bow the knee and give God worship, but also bring presents of golden obedience
	* We must stick at no service and fight under the banner of God's gospel

**7. The role of the wise men**
	* The wise men who came to Christ did not only bow the knee, but also presented Him with gold and myrrh
	* We must do the same and bring presents of golden obedience to God

**8. The relationship between God and His people**
	* God is not only our benefactor, but also our founder
	* We are like rivers that come from the sea and empty our streams into the sea again
	* We should live to Him and give Him glory, since we live by Him

**9. The purpose of the godly**
	* The godly are the lively organs of God's praise
	* They are formed by God for His glory and should show forth His praise
	* They are a royal priesthood, adopted by God to show forth His praise

**10. The importance of giving glory to God**
	* We should give glory to God because He gives us our being and life
	* We should give glory to God because He has made all things for His own glory
	* We should give glory to God because His glory has intrinsic value and excellence.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Praising God**
	+ Praising God is one of the highest and purest acts of true religion
	+ It exalts God in the eyes of others and spreads his fame and renown
	+ Believers are called "temples of God" and their tongues praising God are like the organs in God's spiritual temple sounding

• **Glorifying God through Praise**
	+ Praising God is a way to display the trophies of his excellency
	+ It is a way to honor God and bring glory to his name
	+ Many people are full of murmuring and discontent, but few bring glory to God through praise

• **Glorifying God through Zeal**
	+ Zeal is a mixed affection of love and anger for God
	+ It carries forth our love to God and our anger against sin in an intense degree
	+ Christians fired with zeal take a dishonor done to God worse than an injury done to themselves

• **Glorifying God in Natural Actions**
	+ We glorify God in our natural actions, such as eating and drinking
	+ We should take our food as medicine to heal the decays of nature and be fit for the service of God
	+ We should not use food as fuel for lust, but as help to duty

• **Glorifying God in Civil Actions**
	+ We glorify God in our civil actions, such as buying and selling
	+ We should observe the golden maxim of doing to others as we would have them do to us
	+ We should not falsify the balances or exact more than the commodity is worth

• **Glorifying God by Laboring to Draw Others to God**
	+ We glorify God by seeking to convert others and make them instruments of glorifying God
	+ We should be both diamonds and magnets, shining with the luster of grace and attracting others to Christ

• **Glorifying God through Suffering**
	+ We glorify God in a high degree when we suffer for God and seal the gospel with our blood

	+ We should renew our rent to God by praising and glorifying him
	+ We should be zealous for God's name and take a dishonor done to God worse than an injury done to ourselves

• **The Consequences of Not Glorifying God**
	+ Many people are full of murmuring and discontent, but few bring glory to God through praise
	+ We should not falsify the balances or exact more than the commodity is worth, but rather do to others as we would have them do to us

• **The Reward of Glorifying God**
	+ We glorify God by laboring to draw others to God and making them instruments of glorifying God
	+ We glorify God in a high degree when we suffer for God and seal the gospel with our blood
	+ God's glory shines in the ashes of his martyrs and in the lives of those who glorify him.

• **The Preservation of Scripture**
	+ The church of God has kept public records of heaven, ensuring they have not been lost.

• **The Purity of Scripture**
	+ Scripture has no errors or corruptions, unlike human laws and edicts.
	+ Scripture is pure and sanctifies everything else, as stated in Psalm 119:140 and John 17:17.

• **The Power of Scripture**
	+ Scripture presses holiness and bids us live soberly, righteously, and godly.

• **The Authorship of Scripture**
	+ The author of Scripture must be God himself, as it contains such holiness and purity.
	+ No human or angel could have conceived of the mysteries contained in Scripture.

• **The Predictive Power of Scripture**
	+ Scripture prophesies of things to come, showing the voice of God speaking in it.
	+ Scripture foretells events that would fall out many ages and centuries after, demonstrating its divine origin.

• **The Impartiality of Scripture Writers**
	+ Men of God who wrote the Scriptures do not spare to set down their own failings.
	+ This impartiality is a characteristic of Scripture, unlike human histories that often gloss over flaws.

• **The Evidence of Scripture's Divine Origin**
	+ The preservation, uniqueness, mystery, holiness, purity, power, authorship, predictive power, and impartiality of Scripture all demonstrate its divine origin.

• **Consequences of Ignoring Scripture**
	+ We deviate from the rule and live in contradiction to Scripture
	+ This brings dishonor to true religion
	+ We should lead Bible lives

• **Contending for Scripture**
	+ We should contend for the Word of God
	+ It is a precious jewel that should not be parted with
	+ We should hold fast to instruction and guard it well

• **Importance of Scripture in Our Lives**
	+ The Scripture is a light to our feet and a guide for our walk
	+ It is a book of evidences for heaven
	+ We should be thankful to God for revealing his mind to us through writing

• **Thankfulness for Scripture**
	+ We should be thankful for the Scripture as a sure way of knowing God's mind
	+ It is a mercy that God has made it known to us
	+ We are not left in doubtful suspense, but have an infallible rule to go by

• **Adoring God's Distinguishing Grace**
	+ We should adore God's distinguishing grace if we have felt the power and authority of the Word
	+ We should bless God for giving us his Word and his grace
	+ We should thank God for making the Scripture effective in our lives

	+ The Scriptures principally teach what man is to believe concerning God
	+ God is a Spirit, infinite, eternal, and unchangeable

• **Attributes of God**
	+ God's attributes include wisdom, power, holiness, justice, goodness, and truth
	+ These attributes are essential to God's being

• **Implications of God's Existence**
	+ The belief in God's existence is implied in the question "What is God?"
	+ This implies that there is a God who is a Spirit

• **Expressing God's Nature**
	+ The Scripture expresses that God is a Spirit
	+ This is a fundamental aspect of God's nature

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Existence of God is Demonstrable**
	+ The existence of God can be known through reason and observation of nature.
	+ The notion of a Deity is engraved on man's heart.

• **The Book of Nature**
	+ The book of nature is a demonstration of God's existence.
	+ It is hard for a man to be a natural atheist, as the idea of a Deity is inherent in human nature.

• **God's Works**
	+ The creation of the world and its intricate design demonstrate God's existence.
	+ The beauty and order of the universe are evidence of a wise and supreme maker.

• **The Creation of the World**
	+ The world could not have made itself; there must be a first cause.
	+ The earth's beauty and richness are evidence of God's glory.

• **The Government of the World**
	+ The wise government of all things evinces the existence of God.
	+ God is the great superintendent of the world, guiding all things to their proper end.

• **Providence**
	+ Providence is the queen and governess of the world.
	+ It is the hand that turns the wheel of creation, guiding all things harmoniously.

• **The Order of the Universe**
	+ The excellent order and harmony in the universe demonstrate God's existence.
	+ The sun, rivers, and creatures all act within their spheres, keeping their due bounds.

• **The Support of Creation**
	+ God supports all creatures, keeping them in their constant march.
	+ If God were to withdraw his influence, the wheels of creation would break asunder.

• **The First Mover**
	+ All motion is from something that is unmoveable.
	+ The highest orb, called Primum Mobile, is moved by God.

• **The Evidence of God's Existence**
	+ The evidence of God's existence is seen in the creation, government, and order of the universe.
	+ It is impossible to imagine that the world was not framed by God.

• **The Human Body is a Microcosm**

	+ When a person lives virtuously, conscience approves and gives a sense of satisfaction.

• **The Evidence of God's Existence is Overwhelming**
	+ The evidence of God's existence is overwhelming, from the human body to conscience to fulfilled prophecy.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Sovereignty**
	+ God is the creator and sustainer of all life
	+ As such, he has the right to give us a law and expect our obedience
	+ Submitting to God's will is a way of glorifying him as God

• **God as a Spirit**
	+ God is an immaterial substance, not composed of body and soul
	+ He is a pure, unmixed essence, without extension or parts
	+ His essence is not compounded of body and soul, making him a noble and excellent being

• **Distinguishing God from Other Spirits**
	+ Angels are created spirits, finite and capable of annihilation
	+ Angels are confined spirits, whereas God is an immense Spirit, present everywhere
	+ Angels are ministering spirits, whereas God is a super-excellent Spirit, the Father of spirits

• **God vs. the Soul**
	+ The soul is a spirit, but it is not the same as God's essence
	+ The soul is made intelligible and bears God's likeness, but not his essence
	+ We are made partakers of the divine nature through transformation into God's likeness, not through identity or union with his essence

• **God's Transcendent Excellence**
	+ God is a Spirit of transcendent excellence, unlike angels and human souls
	+ He is the Father of spirits, and his essence is incommunicable

• **Scriptural Evidence**
	+ Scripture describes God as a Spirit, invisible and without bodily shape or substance
	+ God's invisibility and ubiquity are evidence of his spiritual nature
	+ Bodily members ascribed to God are used metaphorically, not literally

• **Impenetrability**
	+ As a Spirit, God is impenetrable and cannot be hurt
	+ Wicked men may imagine evil against God, but they cannot touch his essence
	+ God can hurt his enemies, but they cannot hurt him

• **God's Nature**
	+ God's essence is not capable of dissolution or annihilation
	+ He is the source of all things and has no beginning or end
	+ His nature is perfect and complete, with no defects or limitations

• **God's Power**
	+ As a Spirit, God is not limited by space or time
	+ He is present everywhere and can act in all places at once
	+ His power is not bound by the limitations of human understanding

• **Conclusion**
	+ God's nature as a Spirit is a fundamental aspect of his being
	+ It is essential to understand and acknowledge God's spiritual nature in order to glorify him as God.

	+ God is a Spirit, and cannot be represented by images

	+ God is present in all places
	+ Worshiping God by an image is absurd, as God is present
	+ It is foolish to bow down to a king's picture when the king is present

	+ We must conceive of God spiritually, not physically
	+ We must conceive of God in his attributes, such as holiness, justice, and goodness
	+ We must conceive of God as he is in Christ, who is the image of the invisible God

	+ A Christian who is taken off from earthly things most resembles God

	+ God is spirit, and his worshipers must worship in spirit and in truth (John 4:23-24)
	+ Spiritual worship is pure worship, and comes near to God's own nature

	+ Worshiping God without ceremonies
	+ Worshiping God without the use of Jewish ceremonies or other human-made rituals
	+ Worshiping God with the soul, rather than just the body

	+ God prizes spirit-worship, as it comes near to his own nature
	+ Spiritual worship is acceptable to God, and is what he requires of us

	+ Worshiping God without ceremonies or human-made rituals

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**I. Worshiping God in Spirit**

* Worshiping God in spirit means:
	+ Worshiping with faith in the blood of the Messiah (Heb 10:19)
	+ Worshiping with utmost zeal and intensity of soul
	+ Focusing on the spiritual aspect of duty, which is the soul and quintessence of true religion

**II. Characteristics of Spiritual Worship**

* Spiritual worship is characterized by:
	+ Purity, not pomp (God accepts purity, not outward show)
	+ Repentance through a broken heart, not through outward severities
	+ Thanksgiving through making melody in the heart to the Lord (Eph 5:19)
	+ Prayer through sighs and groans, not through heartless confession (Rom 8:26)

**III. The Importance of Spirit-Worship**

* Spirit-worship is the kind of worship that:
	+ Best pleases God, who is a Spirit (John 4:23)
	+ Is the savory meat that God loves
	+ Is the true holy water, distilled from the penitent eye

**IV. The Sovereignty of God's Spirit**

* God's Spirit is:
	+ A sovereign elixir full of virtue, which can be given in a few drops
	+ A gift that can be received through prayer, asking for the influence of his Spirit

**V. The Need for God's Spirit**

* We need God's Spirit to:
	+ Do any duty in a lively manner
	+ Move swiftly towards heaven
	+ Receive the rewards that God gives

**VI. Spiritual Rewards**

* The rewards that God gives are:
	+ Spiritual blessings, such as Christ, his love, and grace
	+ A crown of glory that does not fade away (1 Pet 5:4)
	+ A sight of God's face in heaven, white robes, and an eternal weight of glory

**VII. The Nature of God's Spirit**

* God's Spirit is:
	+ A Spirit that is incomprehensible, but not the essence of God
	+ A Spirit that can be received through prayer, asking for the influence of his Spirit

**VIII. The Promises of God's Spirit**

* God has made a promise to give his Spirit:
	+ "I will put my Spirit within you" (Ezek 36:27)
	+ We can turn this promise into a prayer, asking for the influence of his Spirit

**IX. The Importance of Prayer**

* Prayer is:
	+ A means of receiving the influence of God's Spirit
	+ A way of moving swiftly towards heaven
	+ A way of receiving the rewards that God gives

**X. The Comfort of Spiritual Rewards**

* The spiritual rewards that God gives are:
	+ Immortal and never-fading
	+ A comfort to Christians in all their labors and sufferings

	+ His essence fills all places, and he must be there in person
	+ He says, "Do not I fill heaven and earth?" (Jer 23:34)

	+ God's throne is in heaven, but also in the humble heart
	+ Neither of these thrones can hold him, as the heaven of heavens cannot contain him

	+ God is present in impure places, but does not mingle with impurity
	+ He is not contaminated by the impurities of created matter
	+ His presence is like the sun shining on a dunghill, without being defiled

	+ God's power is glorious and cannot be set bounds to
	+ He governs all things in his own person, without needing proxies or deputies
	+ He sees all with his own eyes and hears all with his own ears

• **God's Fitness to Judge**
	+ God's omnipresence makes him fit to be the judge of the world
	+ He will do everyone right, as he is present in all places and sees all with his own eyes

	+ God's omnipresence reveals the greatness and immenseness of his majesty
	+ His infinite power and presence are beyond human comprehension

	+ God's omnipresence implies that he is present in all places, governing all things in his own person
	+ He is the one who sees all, hears all, and judges all, without needing proxies or deputies.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**I. God's Omniscience**

* God knows all things, including the deepest thoughts and intentions of humans.
	+ He knows the thoughts of man (Psalm 94:11)
	+ He knows the future, including all contingencies (Daniel 2:28)
	+ He knows the intricacies of human thought and behavior

**II. God's Wisdom**

* God's wisdom is evident in His creation and works.
	+ The creation is a monument of God's power and wisdom (Psalm 104:24)
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the intricate details of the natural world
		- The variety of flowers and fragrances
		- The arrangement of the seasons
		- The balance of light and darkness
		- The mixing of elements (earth and sea)
* God's wisdom is also seen in His redemption of humanity.
	+ Redemption is the masterpiece of divine wisdom
	+ God's infinite wisdom planned a way for humanity's salvation
	+ God's justice and mercy are satisfied through the incarnation of Jesus Christ

**III. God's Creation**

* God's creation is a demonstration of His wisdom and power.
	+ The creation is a looking-glass that reflects God's wisdom
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the arrangement of the natural world
		- The sun, moon, and stars
		- The seasons and weather patterns
		- The balance of light and darkness
* God's creation is also a reflection of His love and care for humanity.
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the provision of food and water
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the social order of humanity
		- The interdependence of people and their roles in society

**IV. God's Redemption**

* God's redemption of humanity is a demonstration of His wisdom and love.
	+ Redemption is the masterpiece of divine wisdom
	+ God's infinite wisdom planned a way for humanity's salvation
	+ God's justice and mercy are satisfied through the incarnation of Jesus Christ
* God's redemption is a reflection of His desire to save humanity from sin and death.
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the plan of salvation
	+ God's love is seen in the sacrifice of Jesus Christ for humanity's sins

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **God's Wisdom in Salvation**
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the mystery of Christ being made sin yet knowing no sin.
	+ God's wisdom is seen in the way salvation is applied through faith, not works.
* **The Means of Salvation**
	+ Salvation is applied through the word preached, as faith comes by hearing.
	+ God's wisdom is seen in using weak and humble means to achieve great things.
* **The Power of God's Wisdom**
	+ God's wisdom is seen in using human instruments, like men, to achieve his purposes.
	+ God's wisdom is seen in not giving glory to human instruments, but to himself.
* **The Wisdom of God's Providence**
	+ God's wisdom is seen in effecting great things through small and contemptible means.
	+ God's wisdom is seen in doing his work through means that seem contrary to human understanding.
* **Examples of God's Wisdom in Providence**
	+ God cured the Israelites by a brazen serpent, a seemingly insignificant object.
	+ God advanced Joseph's position by throwing him into a pit and selling him into Egypt.
	+ God made Israel victorious by lessening Gideon's army and making them rely on God.
	+ God brought his people out of Egypt by stirring up the Egyptians to hate them.
	+ God saved Jonah by letting a fish swallow him up and then spitting him out.
	+ God saved Paul and his companions by wrecking the ship they were on.
	+ God often makes the enemy do his work, and uses persecution to grow and flourish the church.

Here are 10 bullets and sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Wisdom of God in Affliction**
	+ God can use even the most desperate evils to work for the good of his children (Exodus 1:10)
	+ Afflictions can purify and prepare God's people for heaven (2 Corinthians 4:17)
	+ Hard times can hasten the growth of spiritual fruit (2 Corinthians 4:17)
* **God's Wisdom in Using Sin**
	+ God permits sin, but does not approve it (Hebrews 12:4)
	+ God has a hand in the action, but not in the sin itself (Acts 4:27-28)
	+ Men's sinful actions can carry out God's work, even if they don't intend to (Romans 11:11-12)
* **God's Wisdom in Desperate Cases**
	+ God loves to show his wisdom when human help and wisdom fail (Psalm 107:1-3)
	+ God can deliver his people even in the darkest of times (Psalm 107:1-3)
	+ God can melt away the spirits of his enemies or find them other work to do (Joshua 2:24)
* **God's Wisdom in Befooling Wise Men**
	+ God can use the wisdom of men against them (Psalm 94:8-10)
	+ God can counterplot politicians and use their own wit to undo them (Esther 7:10)
	+ God can make the snares that men lay for others catch themselves (Psalm 7:15-16)
* **The Purpose of God's Wisdom**
	+ To show his power and glory (Exodus 1:10)
	+ To bring about the advancement of the gospel (Philippians 1:12)
	+ To bring about the salvation of his people (2 Corinthians 4:17)

Here are 5 bullets with 2 sub-bullets each, summarizing the text:

* **Resting in God's Wisdom**
	+ God's wisdom is infinite and unsearchable, and we should rest in it, rather than trying to understand it.
	+ We should trust that God sees what is best for us, even if it doesn't make sense to us.
* **Resting in God's Wisdom in Times of Spiritual Comfort**
	+ God may sometimes withhold spiritual comfort to keep us humble and prevent pride.
	+ It's better to lack comfort and be humble than to have it and be proud.
* **Resting in God's Wisdom in Times of Bodily Weakness**
	+ God may allow us to be weak in body to make us stronger in faith.
	+ Sickness and weakness can be a means of purging sin and leading to spiritual growth.
* **Resting in God's Wisdom in Times of Uncertainty**
	+ When we don't understand God's providences, we should rest in his wisdom and trust that he knows what he's doing.
	+ God's ways may seem mysterious, but he is always working for our good.
* **Resting in God's Wisdom in Times of Trial**
	+ God may allow us to experience trials and difficulties to test our faith and make us stronger.
	+ We should trust that God is working in our lives, even when we can't see it.

	+ God's power cannot be stopped or hindered

	+ We should tremble before him, knowing his power to cast our souls and bodies into hell

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Power is Not for the Wicked**
	+ God's power is not a relief for the wicked
	+ They have no union with God and therefore no warrant to claim his power
	+ God's power is not engaged to help those who fight against him

• **God's Power is Against the Wicked**
	+ God's power will not be the sinner's shield to defend him
	+ God's power will bind the sinner in chains
	+ God's power serves to revenge the wrong done to his mercy

• **The Unbeliever is in a Dreadful Condition**
	+ God's power is engaged against the unbeliever
	+ It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God

• **The Power of God is Questioned by Many**
	+ Many doubt God's power, not just his will
	+ This unbelief is a great affront to God
	+ Examples of those who doubted God's power include:
		- Israel in the wilderness
		- Martha, who doubted Christ's power to raise Lazarus
		- Moses, who limited God's power through unbelief

• **Doubting God's Power Leads to Indirect Courses**
	+ Those who doubt God's power often take indirect courses

	+ It is like the sun, which has substance, beams, and heat, yet is one sun

• **The Trinity: three substances, one essence**
	+ The three persons in the Trinity are distinguished, but not divided
	+ They are three substances, yet one essence
	+ This is a mystery that cannot be fully explained by human language

	+ The two natures in Christ are a wonder, yet but one person

• **The Trinity: a similitude**
	+ The Trinity can be compared to the sun, which has substance, beams, and heat, yet is one sun
	+ Just as the beams are begotten by the sun and the heat proceeds from both, yet are not divided, so the three persons in the Trinity are one God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Unity in Trinity**
	+ The Unity of the persons in the Godhead consists of two things
	+ Identity of essence: the three persons are of the same divine nature and substance

	+ The three persons in the Trinity are God the Father, Jesus Christ, and the Holy Spirit
	+ Each person has a unique role and relationship with the others

• **God the Father**
	+ The first person in the Trinity, not in dignity but in order
	+ Has no essential perfection that the other persons do not have
	+ Has a priority, not a superiority, over the other persons

• **Jesus Christ**
	+ The second person in the Trinity, begotten of the Father before all time
	+ Eternal generation of the Son of God
	+ Became human as Jesus Christ, our redeemer

	+ The baptism of Jesus, where the Holy Spirit descended like a dove and the Father spoke from heaven

	+ Confutes the Socinians, who deny the Divinity of Jesus Christ

	+ Without the Trinity, man's salvation cannot be wrought out

• **Relationship between the Persons**
	+ The three persons are united and one with another
	+ Each person has a unique role and relationship with the others

• **Implications of the Trinity**
	+ Jesus Christ is co-equal with God the Father
	+ The Holy Spirit is essential for believers to receive the holy unction and be sealed to the day of redemption

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**1. God's Creation**
* God created the world and all its wonders
	+ The heavens, sun, stars, sea, air, and earth all show God's glory
	+ God's creation is a book that we can read and observe

**2. Our Relationship with God**
* We are God's creation and owe ourselves to him
	+ We should serve and obey God, who gives us life and everything we have
	+ God made everything for man's service, including the grain, animals, and birds

**3. Our Humble Origins**
* Our bodies were made from dust, and that dust came from nothing
	+ This should keep us humble and remind us of our dependence on God
	+ Our bodies are made of air and dust, and will eventually return to dust

**4. Our Original Image**
* We were created in God's image, but lost it through sin
	+ We now have the devil's image in pride, malice, and envy
	+ We should strive to restore God's image in us through knowledge, righteousness, and holiness

**5. God's Providence**
* God's providence refers to his wise and powerful government of his creatures and their actions
	+ God's providence is an ongoing work, not just a one-time creation event
	+ God's kingdom rules over all, including his providential kingdom

**6. The Nature of Providence**
* There is no such thing as chance or blind fate
	+ God's providence is a real and active force in the world
	+ God is always at work, even in the smallest details of our lives

**7. Maxims of Providence**
* God's providence is wise and good
	+ God's plans are always for our good, even if we don't understand them
	+ God's providence is not limited to just a few people or events, but is a universal force

**8. God's Rest from Creation**
* God rested from the works of creation, but continues to work through providence
	+ God's rest is not a sign of inactivity, but rather a sign of his ongoing work

**9. The Scope of Providence**
* God's providence extends to all areas of life, including the smallest details
	+ God is not just a distant creator, but an active participant in our lives
	+ God's providence is not limited to just a few areas of life, but is a comprehensive force

**10. Our Response to Providence**
* We should respond to God's providence with gratitude and obedience
	+ We should recognize God's hand in all things and give him the praise due to him
	+ We should strive to live in accordance with God's will and plans for our lives.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Definition of Providence**
	+ Providence is God's ordering of all outcomes and events of things
	+ It is distinct from God's decrees, which ordain things that shall happen
	+ Providence orders things after the counsel of God's will

• **Purpose of Providence**
	+ The ultimate end of all God's actings is his own glory
	+ Providence is the means by which God achieves his glory
	+ It is the center where all the lines of providence meet

• **Scope of Providence**
	+ God's providence reaches to all places, persons, and affairs
	+ It is not limited to any particular location or group of people

• **Providence in Places**
	+ God's presence is everywhere, from heaven to earth to sea
	+ He is not limited by physical boundaries or distances

• **Providence in Persons**
	+ God takes care of every saint in particular
	+ He provides for their needs and shields them from dangers
	+ He is especially concerned with the well-being of the elect

• **Providence in the Lives of Saints**
	+ God provides for the saints' physical needs
	+ He strengthens them in their weakness
	+ He supplies all their needs out of his alms basket

• **Examples of Providence**
	+ God provided for the Protestants in Rochelle by sending a large number of small fish to feed them
	+ He provided for the prophet Elijah by sending a raven to bring him sustenance
	+ He provided for the Virgin Mary by sending wise men from the east with costly gifts

• **God's Care for His People**
	+ He watches over those who fear him and rely on his unfailing love
	+ He rescues them from death and keeps them alive in times of famine
	+ He sets a life-guard of angels about them to shield them from dangers

• **God's Providence in Times of Trouble**
	+ He delivers the righteous from every trouble
	+ He protects them from harm and keeps their bones from being broken
	+ He collects their tears in a bottle and records each one in his book

• **God's Providence in the World**
	+ He is the eye that sees and the hand that turns all the wheels in the universe
	+ He is like a pilot who steers the ship of the whole creation
	+ He is the queen and governess of the world, guiding all events to his own glory.

	+ God's providence reaches all places and persons
	+ It is not limited to specific individuals or locations

	+ We may not always understand why things happen
	+ But we can trust that God's providence is always good and wise

• **God's Providence is a Mystery**
	+ God's providence is a mystery that we cannot fully understand
	+ We can trust that God is always working for our good
	+ But we must not try to understand the details of God's providence.

• **God's providences are mixed**
	+ Both good and bad things can happen in life
	+ Examples: Israel's pillar of cloud, Joseph's imprisonment
	+ God's providences are like a mixture of sweet and bitter

• **The same action can be good or evil**
	+ Depending on the source (God or men)
	+ Examples: Joseph's sale into Egypt, Shimei's cursing of David
	+ God's providence can bring good out of evil actions

• **God's providence is chequer-work**
	+ Intermingled with both good and bad things
	+ Examples: Israel's pillar of cloud, Asher's shoes of brass
	+ God's providence is a mixture of mercy and judgment

• **God's providence is necessary for creation**
	+ Without it, creation would be dissolved
	+ Examples: The world would be in chaos without God's providence
	+ God's providence infuses comfort and virtue into everything

• **We should admire God's providence**
	+ It keeps the world running smoothly
	+ Examples: Our clothes, food, and other comforts
	+ God's providence deserves our admiration and gratitude

• **We should submit to divine providence**
	+ Do not murmur or find fault with God's works
	+ Examples: Quarreling with God's providence is a sin
	+ We should trust in God's wisdom and providence

• **God's providence is his master-wheel**
	+ It turns the world and brings about his purposes
	+ Examples: God's providence is behind all events
	+ We should trust in God's providence and submit to it

• **God's providence is a mystery**
	+ We may not understand why things happen
	+ Examples: Joseph's imprisonment, Shimei's cursing of David
	+ We should trust in God's wisdom and providence even when we don't understand

• **We should learn to trust God in all circumstances**
	+ Trust God even when things seem to be going against him
	+ Examples: David's pursuit by Saul, Paul's shipwreck
	+ Trust in God's providence and submit to his will.

Here are 10 concise bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's sovereignty in providence**
	+ God's providence is a manifestation of His glory
	+ He brings His glory out of all things, even in difficult circumstances

• **Acquiescing in God's will**
	+ We should learn to submit to God's providence and will
	+ We should be content with God's rule over the world

• **Believing in God's goodness**
	+ As Christians, we should believe that all of God's providences will conspire for our good at last
	+ We should trust that God's providences will work together for the good of the elect (Rom 8:28)

• **Understanding God's providence**
	+ God's providences may seem dark and difficult to understand
	+ But we should trust that they will ultimately lead to our good

• **The purpose of affliction**
	+ Affliction may be painful and difficult, but it can be used by God to prepare us for a kingdom (Heb 12:10-11)
	+ Poverty and affliction can starve our sins and prepare us for a kingdom

• **Antidote to fear**
	+ We should not fear the outcome of things, because God is in control
	+ God's providence limits the power of men and prevents them from going further than He permits

• **Thankfulness**
	+ We should be thankful for God's merciful providence
	+ We should recognize that our daily needs are met by God's providence

• **Comfort in God's providence**
	+ God's providence is a comfort to us in times of difficulty
	+ We can trust that God's providence is working for our good, even when we don't understand it

• **God's providence in the church**
	+ God's providence reaches into the church and works for its good
	+ We should trust that God's providence is working to build up the church and prepare it for its ultimate glory

• **The miraculous nature of God's providence**
	+ God's providence is a manifestation of His free grace
	+ We should recognize that God's providence is a miraculous and wonderful thing that deserves our praise and thanksgiving

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **God's Providence in the Church**
	+ God watches over his church continually, watering it with blessings and guarding it day and night.
	+ God preserves his church in the midst of enemies, just as a spark remains alive in the ocean or a flock of sheep remains safe among wolves.
* **God's Miraculous Conduct of Providence**
	+ God led the Israelites by a pillar of fire, gave them manna from heaven, and water from the rock.
	+ God saves his church strangely, often in unexpected ways.
* **God's Unexpected Mercies**
	+ God gives unexpected mercies to his church, such as restoring the exiles to Jerusalem and raising up Queen Esther to save the Jews.
	+ God works in ways that are contrary to human expectations, often bringing low what seems strong and raising up what seems weak.
* **God's Use of Contraries**
	+ God raises his church by bringing it low, just as the blood of the martyrs has watered the church and made it more fruitful.
	+ God grows his church by cutting it back, just as a plant grows by pruning.
* **God's Use of the Enemy**
	+ God can make the enemy do his work, such as setting the armies of Ammon, Moab, and Mount Seir against each other.
	+ God can use the enemy's hand to accomplish his purposes, such as sending away the Israelites laden with jewels.
* **The Mystery of God's Providence**
	+ We can only see glimpses of God's providence on earth, but in heaven we will see the full picture.
	+ We will see how all of God's providences contributed to our salvation and helped to fulfill his promises.
* **The Covenant of Works**
	+ God entered into a covenant of life with Adam and all mankind, upon condition of perfect obedience.
	+ God forbade Adam from eating the tree of knowledge of good and evil, upon pain of death.
* **The Representative Nature of Adam**
	+ Adam was a public person and the representative of the world.
	+ The covenant of works was made with Adam and all mankind, because Adam represented the world.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**I. God's Covenant with Adam**

* **God's Sovereignty**
	+ God is the great Monarch of heaven and earth
	+ He imposed terms of a covenant on Adam
* **The Covenant**
	+ God commanded Adam not to eat from the tree of knowledge
	+ Adam was free to eat from all other trees in the garden
	+ God tested Adam's obedience

**II. The Covenant of Works**

* **The Law**
	+ God gave Adam a law to follow
	+ The law was written in Adam's heart
	+ Adam had power to keep the law
* **The Promise and Threat**
	+ The promise: "Do this and live"
	+ The threatening: "When you eat of it you will surely die"
* **God's Motivation**
	+ God gave Adam a stock of grace to trade with
	+ Adam's fault for not keeping the law
	+ God foresaw Adam's transgression, but still gave him a law

**III. The Form of the First Covenant**

* **By Works**
	+ The covenant of works required perfect obedience
	+ Adam was to live up to the whole breadth of the moral law
* **Not Required for Justification**
	+ Works are not required for justification in the covenant of grace
	+ Works are required as an attestation of love to God
* **The Spirit's Co-Working**
	+ Our obedience is not our own working, but the Spirit's co-working
	+ The Spirit guides and helps us to obey God's law

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Covenant of Works**
	+ Was based on Adam's inherent righteousness
	+ Required perpetual obedience to the law
	+ Had no mercy in case of failure
	+ Left Adam with fears and doubts about his ability to stand

• **The Weakness of the Covenant of Works**
	+ Rested on Adam's mutable nature
	+ Made him his own pilot, with no guarantee of success
	+ Left him vulnerable to temptation and sin

• **The Consequences of Breaking the Covenant**
	+ Adam's condition became deplorable and desperate
	+ He was left helpless, with no place for repentance
	+ The justice of God was offended, setting all other attributes against him

• **The Condescension of God**
	+ God made a covenant with Adam, despite his sinful nature
	+ This was a sign of friendship and royal favor
	+ God bound himself to Adam, giving him life in case of obedience

• **Adam's Glorious Condition**
	+ He was placed in the garden of God, called paradise
	+ He had access to all the trees, precious stones, and metals
	+ He was a king, with all creation bowing to him

• **Adam's Fall**
	+ He was unable to stand in his own strength
	+ His fall shows us how unable we are to stand in our own strength
	+ We need more strength than our own to uphold us

• **The Condition of Unbelievers**
	+ They continue under the curse of the first covenant
	+ They are under the law, with no mercy in case of failure
	+ They are in a sad condition, with no hope of relief

• **The Importance of Faith**
	+ Faith entitles us to the mercy of God
	+ It is the means by which we are saved from the curse of the law

• **The Nature of Man**
	+ Man is mutable, with a power to stand and a power to fall
	+ He is his own pilot, with no guarantee of success
	+ He needs more strength than his own to uphold him

• **The Need for a New Covenant**
	+ The covenant of works was broken by sin
	+ A new covenant was needed to provide relief from the curse of the law
	+ This new covenant would be based on God's mercy and grace, rather than man's inherent righteousness.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The First Covenant**
	+ Men are under the power of their sins and the curse of the first covenant
	+ If they die in that condition, they are damned to eternity

• **The Second Covenant**
	+ God entered into a new covenant with men, called a covenant of grace
	+ It is bespangled with promises and is made in Christ
	+ It is surer and cannot be reversed

• **The Difference between the Two Covenants**
	+ In the first covenant, we had a power of standing, but in the second, we have an impossibility of falling finally
	+ The second covenant is based on faith, not on our inherent goodness or merit

• **The Consequences of Rejecting the Second Covenant**
	+ Those who look for righteousness and salvation by their own power or merit are under the covenant of works
	+ They are bound to keep the whole law and are condemned if they fail

• **The Benefits of the Second Covenant**
	+ We are heirs of the covenant of grace and are in a better state than before
	+ We stand in the strength of Christ and are safe from the justice of God

• **The Nature of Sin**
	+ Sin is any lack of conformity to the law of God or transgression of it
	+ Sin is a violation or transgression that goes beyond our bounds

• **The Characteristics of Sin**
	+ Sin is just, holy, and good, but it is also evil and accursed
	+ Sin is compared to the venom of serpents and the stench of sepulchers

• **The Origin of Sin**
	+ Sin fetches its pedigree from hell and is of the devil
	+ Satan was the first actor of sin and the first tempter to sin

• **The Evil Nature of Sin**
	+ Sin is a defiling thing that pollutes the soul
	+ Sin makes the soul red with guilt and black with filth

• **The Importance of Recognizing Sin**
	+ We should labor by faith to get into the second covenant of grace
	+ We should see the ugly face of sin and not be deceived by its pleasing color

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sin is compared to a plague-sore**

	+ Sin is like a mule that kicks its mother after she has given it milk

	+ Sin makes a person act wickedly and foolishly

• **Sin is irrational**
	+ Sin makes a person prefer the less before the greater
	+ Sin causes a person to lose heaven for the satisfying of a lust

	+ Sin makes the Holy Spirit mourn, like a dove

	+ Sin makes a person act with malice and hypocrisy

• **Sin is a serious offense against God**
	+ Sin strikes at the very Deity and would unthrone God
	+ Sin would un-God him if the sinner could help it

	+ Sin is a source of great suffering and hardship

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**The Dangers of Sin**

* **Sin is a deadly evil**
	+ Brings final damnation if unrepented
	+ Causes corruption in the soul
	+ Leads to the "second death" (Rev 20:14)

	+ Teaches us to hate sin and avoid it at all costs

* **Sin is a Stain on the Soul**
	+ A sinner is the greatest self-denier, denying themselves a part in heaven
	+ Sin brings a sting in the conscience and a curse in the estate

	+ Affliction is like a rip in a coat, but sin is a stab at the heart

**Adam's Sin**

* **The Sin of Adam**
	+ Adam's sin was eating the forbidden fruit (Gen 3:3)
	+ This sin led to the fall of humanity from their estate of innocence
* **The Nature of Adam's Fall**
	+ Adam's fall was voluntary, with free-will being a sufficient shield against temptation
	+ Adam gave away his power and allowed himself to be deceived into sin

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Adam's Fall**
	+ Adam fell from paradise the same day he was created
	+ Reasons for this include:
		- Satan's temptation was a "murdering temptation" that occurred soon after Adam's creation
		- Adam had not yet eaten from the tree of life, which suggests he was not yet settled in paradise

• **Adam's Time in Paradise**
	+ Adam did not stay long in paradise
	+ The Hebrew word for "abide" means to stay or lodge all night, suggesting Adam did not even spend one night in paradise

• **Lessons from Adam's Fall**
	+ From Adam's fall, we learn the weakness of human nature
	+ If Adam, in a state of integrity, quickly fell, how much more vulnerable are we to sin when we are at our worst?

• **The Dangers of Being Left to Oneself**
	+ Adam fell when left to himself, without God's guidance
	+ We are like a ship in a storm without a pilot or anchor, and are prone to fall if left to ourselves

• **The Sin of Adam and Eve**
	+ The sin by which Adam and Eve fell was eating the forbidden fruit
	+ The occasion of this sin was the serpent's temptation

• **The Serpent's Temptation**
	+ The serpent's temptation was a result of Satan's subtlety and deceit
	+ Satan's lies included:
		- "You shall not surely die"
		- "God envies your happiness"
		- "You shall be as gods"

• **Satan's Character**
	+ Satan is a liar and a murderer
	+ He is subtle and deceitful in his temptations

• **The Timing of Satan's Temptation**
	+ Satan set upon Adam and Eve quickly, before they were confirmed in their obedience
	+ This suggests that Satan is always looking for opportunities to tempt and deceive

• **The Consequences of Sin**
	+ Adam's sin led to his expulsion from paradise
	+ We are also prone to sin and fall if we are not kept by a greater power than our own

• **God's Providence**
	+ God puts underneath his everlasting arms to protect us from sin and its consequences
	+ We can pray to God, "Lord, do not leave me to myself!" and ask for his strength to be made perfect in our weakness.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Satan's subtlety in tempting Adam**
	+ Satan tempted Eve first because she was less able to resist
	+ He knew that if he could prevail with her, she would easily draw her husband Adam
	+ Satan's subtlety was in tempting Adam through his wife, making it less suspected

• **Satan's method of temptation**
	+ Satan assaulted Eve's faith by persuading her that God had not spoken truth
	+ He told her, "You shall not surely die" (Gen 3:4), weakening her faith
	+ Once Eve's faith was shaken, she yielded to temptation

• **Satan's cruelty in tempting**
	+ Satan cruelly tempted Adam, dethroning him and bringing him and his posterity under a curse
	+ Satan has an implacable antipathy against mankind, making reconciliation impossible

• **The sin of Adam**
	+ Adam's sin was eating the forbidden fruit
	+ This sin was heinous in respect of the person who committed it (Adam)

• **Aggravation of Adam's sin**

	+ He was illuminated with knowledge and embellished with holiness, making his disobedience more notable

• **Dreadfulness of the effect**
	+ Adam's sin had a profound impact on humanity, bringing a curse upon all his posterity
	+ The sin was a chain with many links, including unbelief, disobedience, and more

• **Unbelief as a component of Adam's sin**
	+ Adam and Eve did not believe what God had spoken was truth
	+ They doubted God's warning that they would surely die if they ate the forbidden fruit

• **The nature of Adam's sin**
	+ Adam's sin was a big-bellied sin, a chain with many links
	+ It was a voluminous sin, with many sins twisted together in it

• **The consequences of Adam's sin**
	+ Adam's sin brought a curse upon all his posterity
	+ The sin had a profound impact on humanity, affecting all of us

• **The significance of Adam's sin**
	+ Adam's sin was a sin against an infinite God
	+ It was a heinous sin, with many aggravating factors, making it a significant event in human history

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Adam's Sin: Unbelief**
	+ They called God a liar by not believing Him
	+ They believed the devil rather than God

• **Unthankfulness**
	+ Adam was ungrateful for the mercies God had given him

	+ He ate from the tree of knowledge of good and evil

	+ He pried into God's secrets and tasted forbidden fruit

• **Presumption**
