	+ He broke the covenant and showed great presumption

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**1. Original Sin and Its Consequences**

* The sin of Adam had far-reaching consequences, affecting all of humanity.
* The Bible states that "just as sin entered the world through one man, and death through sin, and in this way death came to all men, because all sinned" (Romans 5:12).

**2. Adam as a Representative Person**

* Adam was a representative person, and his actions had consequences for all of humanity.
* When Adam fell, all of humanity fell with him, as stated in Romans 5:12.

**3. Why All of Humanity Fell with Adam**

* The reason all of humanity fell with Adam is because of their relationship to him.
* As children of Adam, we are part of him, and his sin is imputed to us.

**4. How Adam's Sin is Made Ours**

* Adam's sin is made ours through imputation, meaning that his guilt is transferred to us.
* Additionally, the depravity and corruption of Adam's nature is transmitted to us, resulting in original sin.

**5. Original Sin**

* Original sin is the depravity and corruption of Adam's nature that is transmitted to us.
* It is also called the "old man" (Ephesians 4:22) and the "law of sin" (Romans 7:25).

**6. Characteristics of Original Sin**

* Original sin has both privative and positive aspects.
* Privatively, it is the lack of righteousness that should be ours.
* Positively, it is the contamination and defilement of our virgin nature.

**7. The Nature of Original Sin**

* Original sin has become natural to us, and we cannot cease to sin without effort.
* It is an innate principle in us that leads us to sin, even without external temptation.

**8. The Effects of Original Sin**

* Original sin leads to an aversion from good and a propensity to evil.
* We have a natural disgust of holiness and a tendency to oppose that which would promote our happiness.

**9. The Inability to Return to God**

* Since the fall, we have no mind to return to God.
* Our natural bias is towards that which is bad, and we are prone to sin.

**10. The Universality of Sin**

* Even the heathens, by the light of nature, saw that humans are prone to sin.
* The Bible states that "all of us have sinned" (Romans 3:23), and that we are all under the power of sin.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**I. The Nature of Sin**

* **Universal Sin**
	+ Sin is a universal human condition
	+ It affects every part of the soul
* **Strong Appetite for Sin**
	+ Humans have a strong desire for sin
	+ Despite God's efforts to stop them, they continue to sin

**II. The Universality of Original Sin**

* **Depravity of the Mind**
	+ Original sin has darkened the understanding
	+ Humans are ignorant of God and judge things incorrectly
	+ Pride, disdainfulness, and prejudice are common
* **Depravity of the Heart**
	+ The heart is "deadly wicked" and "deceitful"
	+ It is full of lusts, obdurateness, infidelity, and hypocrisy
* **Depravity of the Will**
	+ The will is in opposition to God's will
	+ Humans have a rooted enmity against holiness
* **Depravity of the Affections**
	+ Affections are set on wrong objects
	+ Love and joy are focused on sin and the creature

**III. The Adhesion of Original Sin**

* **Inbred Corruption**
	+ Original sin is deeply ingrained in humans
	+ It cannot be easily shaken off
* **Comparison to a Wild Fig-Tree**
	+ Original sin is like a wild fig-tree that grows on a wall
	+ It has roots that cannot be completely eradicated
* **Sin as an Indweller**
	+ Original sin is not just a temporary condition
	+ It is a permanent resident in the human heart

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Original sin is a pervasive evil spirit**
	+ Haunts us wherever we go
	+ Retards and hinders us in exercising God's worship

• **Original sin retards our spiritual progress**
	+ Causes dullness and deadness in religion
	+ Rocks us asleep in duty
	+ Prevents us from doing good

• **Original sin is a hidden but powerful force**
	+ Latent in the soul, like a spring running underground
	+ Can break forth unexpectedly, like a storm

• **Original sin is a corrupting influence**
	+ Mixes with our duties and graces
	+ Defiles our prayers and tears
	+ Causes us to stumble and fall

• **Original sin is a vigorous and active principle**
	+ Excites and stirs us up to evil
	+ Is like quicksilver, always in motion
	+ Sets our imagination and actions plotting evil

• **Original sin is the cause of all actual sin**
	+ Is the kindling-wood of sin
	+ Is the womb in which all actual sins are conceived
	+ Leads to murders, adulteries, and other evils

• **Original sin is not perfectly cured in this life**
	+ Though grace subdues sin, it does not wholly remove it
	+ We still have the remainders of sinful flesh
	+ We are unlike Christ, who is sinless

• **Original sin is a corrupting influence on our graces**
	+ Mixes with our faith, zeal, and humility
	+ Causes unbelief, lukewarmness, and pride
	+ Inhibits our spiritual growth and progress

• **Original sin is a powerful and persistent force**
	+ Can break forth suddenly and scandalously
	+ Can reveal itself in even the holiest of men
	+ Can lead to great evil and destruction

• **Original sin requires the atonement of Christ's blood**
	+ Makes atonement for our holy things
	+ Covers our sins and makes us acceptable to God
	+ Is necessary for our salvation and spiritual growth

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Original Sin Remains After Regeneration**
	+ The Spirit continues to weaken and hew down sin in the godly
	+ However, the root of original sin remains
	+ This is a sea that will not be dried up in this life
* **Why God Leaves Original Sin**
	+ To show the power of his grace in the weakest believer
	+ To make us long for heaven, where there will be no sin
	+ To humble us and pull down the plumes of pride
* **Original Sin vs. Sinless Perfection**
	+ The Libertines and Quakers claim to be without sin, but this is not true
	+ Original sin is inherent in us while we live, and even the best of us have its seeds
	+ Paul complained of a "body of death" and said there is not a just man who lives and sins not
* **The Nature of Sin in Believers**
	+ Believers are dead spiritually to sin
	+ They are dead to the guilt and power of sin, but not yet to its presence
	+ The love of sin is crucified, but not yet fully eradicated
* **The Consequences of Original Sin**
	+ It is an active principle in us, stirring us up to evil
	+ It is worse than actual sin, as the fountain is more than the stream
	+ It defiles everything we do, and even the purest water cannot cleanse it
* **The Purpose of Original Sin**
	+ To humble us and make us look up to heaven for help
	+ To make us beg for Christ's blood to wash away guilt and his Spirit to mortify sin
	+ To make us walk with continual jealousy and watchfulness over our hearts
* **The Need for Watchfulness**
	+ The sin of our nature is like a sleeping lion, waiting to be awakened
	+ We need to walk watchfully, lest we fall into scandalous evils
	+ A wandering heart needs a watchful eye
* **The Misery of the Fall**
	+ All mankind lost their original righteousness and became sinful
	+ This is the misery of the estate into which man fell
	+ It is a state of sin and corruption that affects all of humanity
* **The Importance of Humility**
	+ Original sin should humble us and make us aware of our need for God's grace
	+ We should be deeply humbled for our sin and its consequences
	+ We should recognize that even the best of us have the seeds of original sin
* **The Hope of Redemption**
	+ Death will make a conquest of sin, and we will be free from its power
	+ We should look forward to the day when sin will be fully eradicated
	+ We should continue to beg for God's grace and help in our daily lives

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**I. Privative Misery**

* We have lost communion with God due to original sin
	+ Adam was God's familiar friend, but sin put us out of favor
	+ We lost God's image and acquaintance

**II. Positive Misery**

* We are under the power of Satan
	+ We are enslaved to Satan, who is a hater of mankind
	+ We are subject to Satan's command, like a donkey at the command of a driver
* We are heirs of God's wrath
	+ We are under God's judgment and condemnation
* We are subject to all the miseries of this life
	+ We suffer physical, emotional, and spiritual pain
* We are exposed to hell and damnation
	+ We are destined for eternal punishment if we do not repent

**III. Satan's Power Over Us**

* Satan rules our understanding
	+ He blinds us to the error of our ways and leads us into sin
	+ We are ignorant of God's laws and ways
* Satan rules our will
	+ He tempts us and draws us into sin
	+ We obey Satan's lusts and disobey God's laws

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The State of Sin**
	+ Sinners are slaves to Satan and willingly obey him
	+ They refuse to take their freedom from sin
	+ They kiss their fetters, indicating a willingness to remain in bondage

• **The Gospel Offers Freedom**
	+ The gospel proclaims a jubilee to captives, freeing them from Satan's tyranny
	+ Through Christ, believers are made free from Satan's power
	+ The gospel is a call to repentance and freedom from sin

• **The Misery of Being Children of Wrath**
	+ By nature, humans are children of wrath, heirs of God's displeasure
	+ God's wrath is infinite and dreadful, unlike human wrath
	+ Wrath in God is an act of His holy will, abhorring sin and decreeing punishment

• **The Consequences of Being Children of Wrath**
	+ Believers have no right to the precious promises of God
	+ The promises are like a fountain sealed, inaccessible to those in a state of nature
	+ The sinner is exposed to God's fiery indignation and fearful of His wrath

• **The Curse of Sin**
	+ The sinner is heir to all of God's curses
	+ A curse is written on the sinner's name, soul, estate, and posterity
	+ The ordinances of God are also cursed for the sinner

• **The Reality of God's Wrath**
	+ God's wrath is not a passion, but an act of His holy will
	+ It is infinite and dreadful, unlike human wrath
	+ God's wrath is mingled with affliction, making it torturous

• **The Effect of God's Wrath**
	+ It embitters afflictions in this life
	+ It puts conscience into an agony
	+ It is like a nail in the heart

• **The Importance of Repentance**
	+ Repentance is necessary to get out from under Satan's power
	+ It involves turning from sin to God
	+ It is a call to freedom from sin and Satan's tyranny

• **The Promises of God**
	+ The promises of God are like a tree of life, bearing fruit
	+ They are inaccessible to those in a state of nature
	+ Believers have a right to the promises through faith in Christ

• **The Call to Freedom**
	+ Believers are called to get out from under Satan's jurisdiction
	+ They are called to get their fetters of sin filed off through repentance
	+ They are called to be conquerors, not captives, in Christ.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Curse of God**
	+ Makes every sweet thing bitter
	+ Results in misery and suffering
	+ Is a consequence of original sin

• **Heirs of Wrath**
	+ We are born under God's displeasure
	+ Must labor to re-ingratiate ourselves with God
	+ Can only find shelter in Jesus Christ

• **Outward Miseries**
	+ We are subject to all troubles in life
	+ Comforts below cannot fill the heart
	+ Life is labor and sorrow, with more water of tears than wine of joy

• **The Futility of Life**
	+ All creatures are vanity and emptiness
	+ Our life is a series of disappointments and fears
	+ We are born heir to trouble, not land

• **The Plague of Fear**
	+ Fear is the result of sin
	+ Torment and anxiety are constant companions
	+ We fear loss, poverty, and separation from loved ones

• **Disappointments and Crosses**
	+ We experience disappointments and crosses in life
	+ Where we expect comfort, there is a cross
	+ The earth is filled with violence and wickedness

• **The Fruit of Original Sin**
	+ All troubles and miseries are the result of original sin
	+ Sin has brought death and suffering into the world
	+ We are born with a sinful nature, prone to evil

• **The Deformities of Sin**
	+ Sin has brought deformities and diseases into the world
	+ The heart is hardened and sinful, leading to physical problems
	+ Death is the ultimate result of sin

• **The Dread of Death**
	+ Death is dreadful to nature
	+ Sin has brought death into the world
	+ Adam would not have died if he had not sinned

• **The Misery of Sin**
	+ Sin dissolves the harmony and well-being of the world
	+ We are born under the curse of sin, with no escape
	+ Only Jesus Christ can save us from the wrath of God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**The Miseries of Original Sin**

* **1.1. Loss of Joy**: Original sin exposes us to hell and damnation, banishing us from the beatific presence of God.
* **1.2. Punishment of Sense**: The sinner feels God's wrath, which is penetrating, abiding, and reserved.
* **1.3. Eternal Torment**: Hell is the epitome of misery, with no end to the suffering.
* **1.4. No Escape**: It is impossible to escape the torments of hell.
* **1.5. Forever Separated**: The miseries of hell are forever, with no rest day or night.
* **1.6. God's Wrath**: The breath of the Lord kindles the fire of hell, and there is no way to quench it.
* **1.7. No Rest**: The smoke of their torment ascends up forever and ever.

**The Covenant of Grace**

* **2.1. God's Plan**: God entered into a covenant of grace to deliver the elect from the estate of sin and misery.
* **2.2. The Covenant**: The new covenant is a solemn compact and agreement between God and fallen man.
* **2.3. Names of the Covenant**: It is called the covenant of peace, because it seals up reconciliation between God and humble sinners.
* **2.4. A Covenant of Grace**: It is well-named, because it was of grace that God made it, and it restores us to life through a Redeemer.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Covenant of Grace**
	+ Is a royal charter made up of terms of grace
	+ Includes promises such as God casting sins behind his back and loving us freely
	+ Is a covenant of pure grace, not earned by human merit

• **Why God Enters into a Covenant**
	+ It is out of indulgence, favor, and regard for us
	+ A covenant is a mark of distinction between God's people and heathens
	+ God's entering into a covenant with us is a dignity he puts upon us

• **Differences between the Covenant of Grace and the First Covenant**
	+ The terms of the first covenant were more strict and severe
		- The least failing would have made the covenant null and void
		- The first covenant allowed no remedy for sinners, but the new covenant does

• **The Nature of the Covenant of Grace**
	+ Is based on believing, not working
	+ Requires good works as signs of life, not as conditions of salvation
	+ Works are required as evidences of our love to God, not as grounds of salvation

• **The Condition of the Covenant of Grace**
	+ The main condition is faith
	+ Faith is a gift from God, not something we earn
	+ Faith excludes all glorying in the creature and gives all glory to Christ

• **Why Faith is the Condition of the Covenant of Grace**
	+ To exclude all glorying in the creature
	+ Faith is a humble grace that fetches all from Christ
	+ Faith gives all the glory to Christ, not to human merit

• **The Importance of the Covenant of Grace**
	+ It is a bond that ties us fast to God
	+ It is a solemn promise that God will not break, even when we fail
	+ It provides a remedy for sinners and leaves room for repentance

• **The Relationship between the Covenant of Grace and Works**
	+ Works are required in the covenant of grace, but not as conditions of salvation
	+ Works are required as signs of life, not as grounds of salvation
	+ Works are required to manifest our love to God, not to justify our persons

• **The Role of Repentance in the Covenant of Grace**
	+ Repentance is not the condition of the covenant, but rather a response to the covenant
	+ Repentance is a remedy provided by the new covenant
	+ Repentance leaves room for hope and provides a way for sinners to be reconciled to God

• **The Significance of the Covenant of Grace in Our Lives**
	+ It is a reminder of God's love and favor towards us
	+ It is a bond that ties us fast to God, even when we fail
	+ It provides a way for us to experience God's mercy and forgiveness.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Covenant of Grace**
	+ The covenant of grace is based on faith
	+ Faith is the condition of the covenant
	+ Without faith, one is excluded from the covenant

• **God's Condescension**
	+ God entered into covenant with humanity despite their sin
	+ This is a demonstration of God's amazing goodness and love
	+ The covenant is a marriage covenant, with God making Himself over to us

• **Characteristics of God's Covenant People**
	+ **Humble**: God's people esteem others better than themselves and shrink into nothing in their own thoughts
	+ **Willing**: God's people serve Him willingly and do not grudge Him time spent in worship
	+ **Consecrated**: God's people are separated from the world and sanctified by the Spirit, bearing God's name and image

• **The Importance of Humility**
	+ Pride excludes one from the covenant
	+ God resists the proud and will not take them to be with Himself in glory
	+ Abraham, the father of the faithful, confessed his nothingness and humility

• **The Importance of Willingness**
	+ God's people serve Him willingly and spontaneously
	+ This willingness is from the attractive power of God's Spirit
	+ God accepts willingness without the work, but not the work without willingness

• **The Importance of Consecration**
	+ God's people are separated from the world and sanctified by the Spirit
	+ They bear God's name and image, and are adorned with holiness of heart
	+ Holiness is God's stamp, and without it, He will not own us as His covenant people

• **Exhortation to Those Out of Covenant**
	+ Labor to get into covenant with God
	+ Consider the misery of living and dying outside of covenant with God
	+ There is no mercy or help outside of the covenant

• **The Ark of the Covenant**
	+ The old world would have been glad of an ark
	+ We should be industrious to get within the ark of the covenant
	+ The covenant is a refuge from the storms of life

• **The Consequences of Living Outside the Covenant**
	+ Those who live and die outside of covenant with God have none to go to in an hour of distress
	+ They will be filled with horror and despair, like Saul when he said the Lord had departed

• **The Call to Enter the Covenant**
	+ Consider the benefits of being in covenant with God
	+ Labor to get into covenant and have God for your God
	+ The covenant is a marriage covenant, with God making Himself over to us.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Mercy-Seat**
	+ Placed on the ark to show God's mercy reaches no further than the covenant
	+ Symbolizes the covenant of grace

• **The Covenant of Grace**
	+ Better than the covenant with Adam
		- More friendly and acceptable
		- Accepts sincerity and willingness over deeds

• **The Covenant of Grace (continued)**
	+ Surer than the covenant with Adam

	+ To accept us through Christ's worthiness

• **What Keeps Us from Covenant with God**
	+ Unwillingness, not unworthiness
	+ We must be willing to seek God and break off the covenant with sin

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Covenant is a Branch of God's Love**
	+ God crowns us with love and forgiveness
	+ Sin is pardoned, and all wrath ceases
	+ God appears in mercy, not wrath

• **Temporal Mercies are Fruits of the Covenant**
	+ Wicked men receive mercies by Providence, not covenant
	+ Those in covenant receive mercies sweetened with God's love
	+ They receive health, riches, and blessings with God's love

• **Pleading the Covenant in Times of Need**
	+ Plead the covenant against temptations and harassment
	+ Plead the covenant for deliverance from poverty and hell
	+ Plead the covenant for daily bread and kingdom

• **All Things Cooperate for Good in the Covenant**
	+ Not only golden paths, but also bloody paths are for good
	+ Afflictions humble and purify, and add to the saints' glory
	+ Every wind of Providence blows us nearer to heaven

• **The Covenant is Eternal and Unchangeable**
	+ God's electing love is unchangeable
	+ The covenant is an everlasting covenant, not to be turned away from
	+ God will not forsake the saints, and the saints will not forsake God

• **Union with Christ is Inseparable**
	+ Christ is the head, and believers are the body
	+ This union is like that between God the Father and Christ
	+ It can never be dissolved, and the covenant is never made void

• **Death Perfects the Union with Christ**
	+ Death breaks the union between the body and soul
	+ Death perfects the union between Christ and the soul
	+ The saints desire death as the bride desires the wedding-day

• **Walking in Covenant with God**
	+ Love God with a free and full love
	+ Differ from others in point of life-style
	+ Live as a people in covenant with God

• **The Covenant is a League of Friendship**
	+ God passes by the noble and mighty to choose the dove
	+ God's love is Amor gratiatu s—a free love
	+ God's love is Amor plenus—a full love

• **The Covenant is a Treasure of Blessings**
	+ God gives us the key to all his treasure
	+ God heaps pearls upon us
	+ God settles heaven and earth upon us

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Love and Covenant**
	+ God's covenant with us calls for love and devotion
	+ We should respond to God's love with love and gratitude

• **Walking Holily**

	+ Holiness is what keeps us in communion with God

• **Walking Thankfully**

• **Christ as Savior**
	+ Jesus is the only Savior, who saves us from sin and hell
	+ He is the only one who can save us, as there is no other salvation

	+ He is our elder brother, who has the best right to redeem us

• **Christ as Mediator**
	+ Christ is the Mediator of the new covenant, who reconciles us to God
	+ He takes away the enmity between us and God, and makes peace

	+ Christ is not only a Mediator of reconciliation, but also of intercession
	+ He appears in the presence of God for us, and intercedes on our behalf

	+ Christ is the only one who can save us, as there is no other salvation

	+ Christ is the sum and quintessence of the gospel
	+ He is the wonder of angels, and the joy and triumph of saints

• **Christ's Names and Titles**
	+ Christ has several names and titles in Scripture, including Savior, Redeemer, and Mediator
	+ These names and titles highlight his unique role and importance in our salvation.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**1. Christ's Person**
* Christ's person is amiable, made up of all love and beauty
	+ He is the effigy of his Father, the express image of his person

**2. Christ's Two Natures**
* Christ has two natures: human and divine
	+ His human nature is incarnate, taking on flesh to reveal God's mind

**3. Human Nature**
* Christ's human nature is essential for our salvation
	+ He took on our flesh to know how to pity us and ennoble our human nature
	+ He knows what it is to be faint, sorrowful, and tempted

**4. Divine Nature**
* Christ's divine nature is essential for his mission
	+ He is the mighty God, the fullness of the Godhead, and of the same nature and essence with the Father
	+ He is eternal, omnipresent, and the heart-searcher

**5. Christ's Deity**
* Christ is God, not just a created being
	+ He is worthy of divine worship, creation, and prayer
	+ He is the one to whom faith and trust are given

**6. Christ's Two Natures in One Person**
* Christ has a twofold substance, divine and human, yet not a twofold subsistence
	+ Both natures make but one Christ, a God-man

**7. Christ's Humanity**
* Christ's human nature is essential for his sacrifice
	+ He took on our flesh to suffer and die for our sins

**8. Christ's Divinity**
* Christ's divine nature is essential for his sacrifice
	+ He is the one who was offered in sacrifice, and it was God who was offended and satisfied

**9. Christ's Blood**
* Christ's blood is called the blood of God
	+ It is the blood of a God-man, who was both human and divine

**10. Christ's Person is Amiable**
* Christ's person is amiable, made up of all love and beauty
	+ He is the express image of his Father, and his sacrifice is meritorious because of his divine nature.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Union of Christ's Natures**
	+ Christ's human and divine natures are united in an ineffable manner
	+ There are two natures, yet only one person
	+ The union is not by transmutation or mixture, but by a distinct yet unified person

• **Christ's Perfection in Grace**
	+ Christ has perfection in every grace
	+ He is a panoply, treasury, and storehouse of all heavenly treasure
	+ No saint on earth has this perfection in all graces

• **The Never-Failing Fullness of Christ's Grace**
	+ Christ's grace is never-failing and never abated
	+ He never lost a drop of his holiness
	+ His bow (grace) remained in strength despite attacks from men and devils

• **Christ's Communicative Grace**
	+ Christ's grace is communicative and for others
	+ He diffuses his grace to others, unlike saints who have no grace to spare
	+ His grace is like water in a spring, flowing to those who need it

• **The Richness of Christ's Consolation**
	+ Those with little or no grace can go to Christ for comfort
	+ Christ is a full fountain of grace, pardon, and cleansing
	+ He is a balm of Gilead for those who are sick or polluted

• **Christ as a Treasury of Grace**
	+ Christ is a storehouse of all heavenly treasure
	+ He opens all the treasuries and storehouses of grace for those who come to him
	+ He is a full breast that aches to be drawn upon for grace

• **The Glory of Christ as Mediator**
	+ Christ is a God-man, co-essentially glorious with the Father
	+ Believers see Christ's glory, but others do not
	+ Christ's glory is like the sparkle within the temple, visible only to those who are made priests unto God

• **The Limitations of Saints' Grace**
	+ Saints have not grace to bestow on others
	+ They have only the drops and influences of Christ's grace distilling upon them
	+ They may go to Christ for more grace, but they cannot give it to others

• **The Importance of Admiring Christ's Glory**
	+ Believers should admire the glory of Christ as Mediator
	+ Christ's glory is worthy of our awe and reverence
	+ We should come to Christ with empty vessels, ready to be filled with his grace

• **The Invitation to Come to Christ**
	+ Christ invites us to come to him for grace
	+ He is a full fountain of pardon, cleansing, and healing
	+ We should come to Christ with our empty vessels, ready to be filled with his grace.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**1. The Dignity of Believers**
* Believers are closely related and united to Christ
* Christ's two natures (divine and human) are married together
* Christ's human nature prays for believers, while his divine nature merits for them

**2. The Importance of Looking to Christ**
* Christ is the only one to look to for salvation
* Our own righteousness and moralism are not enough to save us
* We must look to Christ's merits and blood for salvation

**3. The Love of Christ**
* Christ humbled himself to take our flesh and redeem us
* Believers should put Christ in their hearts and have him written there

**4. Christ's Prophetic Office**
* Christ is the great Prophet of his church
* He is the best teacher, making all other teaching effectual
* He teaches to profit and opens the Scriptures to those who follow him

**5. How Christ Teaches**
* Externally, through his Word (e.g. "Your word is a lamp to my feet")
* Inwardly, through the Spirit (e.g. "The natural man receives not the things of God")

**6. The Limitations of Human Understanding**
* The natural man cannot know the deep things of God
* He may have insight into the world, but not into the secrets of the kingdom of heaven

**7. The Importance of Being Taught by Christ**
* Those who are taught by Christ see the secrets of the kingdom of heaven
* They are transformed by the renewing of the mind and understand the inward workings of the Spirit

**8. Christ's Teaching Methods**
* He teaches us to see into our own hearts
* He teaches us to understand the mysteries of the kingdom of heaven

**9. The Role of the Word**
* The Word is a lamp to our feet and a light to our path
* Those who pretend to have a light or revelation above the Word are not taught by Christ

**10. The Result of Being Taught by Christ**
* We are transformed by the renewing of the mind
* We understand the inward workings of the Spirit and see the secrets of the kingdom of heaven

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Christ's Teaching is Unique**
	+ Christ teaches the heart, not just the ear
	+ He works grace, not just knowledge
	+ He gives the love of the truth, not just the truth itself
* **Christ's Teaching Reveals the Heart**
	+ Christ removes the veil of ignorance and illuminates the way to see into one's own heart
	+ He reveals the evil that is in us and the vanity of the creature
	+ He shows us the excellency of unseen realities
* **Christ's Teaching is Different from Other Teaching**
	+ Christ teaches obedience, not just instruction
	+ He makes us willing to learn, not just teaches us what to believe
	+ He gives us a taste of the word, not just knowledge
* **Christ's Teaching is Easy and Effective**
	+ Christ teaches with ease, not with difficulty
	+ He can convert with the least touch of his Spirit
	+ He can convey grace with a word
* **Christ's Teaching is Irresistible**
	+ His grace is irresistible, making the stubborn sinner yield to him
	+ He not only informs the mind, but inclines the will
	+ He removes obstinacy and makes us willing to learn
* **Christ's Teaching Reveals the Vanity of the Creature**
	+ Christ shows us that earthly things are empty and unsatisfying
	+ He reveals that the creature is not commensurate to a heaven-born soul
	+ He teaches us to look beyond the creature to unseen realities
* **Christ's Teaching Reveals the Excellency of Unseen Realities**
	+ Christ gives us a sight of glory and a prospect of eternity
	+ He shows us a better country, a heavenly one
	+ He reveals the delights of angels and the rivers of pleasure
* **Christ's Teaching is Based on Scripture**
	+ Christ's teaching is based on the Bible, specifically the writings of Augustine and the apostle Paul
	+ He teaches us to look at things which are not seen, but to believe in things which are not seen
* **Christ's Teaching is Personal and Transformative**
	+ Christ's teaching is not just a intellectual exercise, but a personal and transformative experience
	+ He teaches us to see ourselves and the world in a new light
	+ He makes us willing to learn and obey, not just to believe
* **Christ's Teaching is a Gift of Grace**
	+ Christ's teaching is a gift of grace, not something we can earn or achieve
	+ He teaches us to see the beauty and wonder of the gospel
	+ He reveals the love and mercy of God to us.

• **Christ's Teaching Style**
	+ He makes his people a "willing people" who prize knowledge

• **The Misery of Man in the State of Nature**

	+ They are in danger of falling into hell at every step

	+ He sees none of the beauty of Christ, and heaven is veiled from him
	+ He is in danger of falling into hell because of his ignorance

• **The Spiritual Darkness**

	+ Men like their condition well enough, and love darkness
	+ This is their dreadful condition until Jesus Christ comes as a prophet to teach them

• **The Happy Condition of the Children of God**
	+ They have Christ to be their prophet, who teaches them of the Lord
	+ He is made to them wisdom, and they see with Christ's eyes
	+ They are enlightened and made to see the beauty of Christ

• **Christ's Teaching Ability**
	+ He can teach the dead, and give the light of life
	+ He quickens those who are dead in sin, and makes them hear his voice
	+ He gives the heat of spiritual life along with his light

• **The Comparison of Christ's Wisdom**
	+ His wisdom is compared to the sun, which enlightens the whole world
	+ He is the great luminary, and in him are hidden all treasures of knowledge
	+ His wisdom is infinite, and far greater than that of Aristotle and Plato

• **The Importance of Christ's Teaching**
	+ He is the only one who can reveal the secrets of Heaven
	+ He is the only one who can give saving knowledge to all the elect
	+ His teaching is essential for the salvation of man.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **See your need of Christ's teaching**
	+ Recognize that you cannot understand spiritual things on your own
	+ Acknowledge that your reason is limited and cannot fathom the deep things of God
	+ See your need for Christ's anointing and teaching, as described in Revelation 3:18

• **Go to Christ to teach you**
	+ Pray to Christ to teach you, as one of the disciples prayed to be taught to pray
	+ Ask Christ to give you a spirit of wisdom and revelation
	+ Ask Christ to teach you in the Word and in the sacrament

• **Christ is willing to teach you**
	+ Jesus Christ entered into the ministry to teach the mysteries of heaven
	+ Christ is angry with those who keep away the key of knowledge
	+ Christ was anointed with the Spirit without measure to anoint us with knowledge

• **Christ can teach anyone**
	+ There is no one so dull or ignorant that Christ cannot teach them
	+ Christ teaches in a way that even the unlearned and lowly can understand
	+ The unlearned men in the Bible rose up and took heaven, knowing the truths of Christ more savingly than the great sages and wise men

• **Wait upon the means of grace**
	+ Wait at the gates of wisdom's door, which are the ordinances of the church
	+ Ministers are teachers under Christ, and Christ speaks to us through them
	+ Wean yourself from the breast of ordinances, and you will not thrive spiritually

• **Christ teaches through his Spirit and ordinances**
	+ Christ teaches by his Spirit, but also uses the ordinances of the church to teach us
	+ Ministers are earthen vessels with lamps within them to light souls to heaven

• **Christ delights in teaching the ignorant**
	+ Christ delights in teaching the dull and ignorant to get himself more glory
	+ The eyes of the blind shall be opened, and the ears of the deaf shall be unstopped

• **Christ is more willing to teach a blind soul than a blind body**
	+ Christ is more willing to work a cure on a blind soul than on a blind body
	+ None in the gospel came to Christ for sight, but he restored their eyesight

• **Christ teaches in a way that is more effective than human teaching**
	+ Christ teaches in a way that is more effective than human teaching, even for the unlearned and lowly
	+ The unlearned men in the Bible knew the truths of Christ more savingly than the great sages and wise men

• **We must go to Christ for teaching**
	+ We must go to Christ for teaching, rather than relying on our own reason or human wisdom
	+ Christ is the only one who can teach us the deep things of God and lead us to salvation

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Christ's Voice in the Ministry**
	+ The word preached is Christ's voice in the mouth of the minister.
	+ Those who refuse to hear Christ speaking in the ministry will be refused by Christ on their deathbed.
* **Using Knowledge to Learn More**
	+ Use your little knowledge well to learn more from Christ.
	+ Christ will teach you more if you walk according to the knowledge you already have.
* **The Importance of Being Thankful**
	+ If you have been taught by Christ savingly, be thankful.
	+ It is an honor to have God as your teacher.
* **Christ's Priestly Office**
	+ Christ executes the office of a priest through his once offering up of himself as a sacrifice.
	+ He reconciles us to God and makes continual intercession for us.
* **Parts of Christ's Priestly Office**
	+ Christ's priestly office has two parts: satisfaction and intercession.
	+ Satisfaction consists of active and passive obedience.
* **Active Obedience**
	+ Christ fulfilled all righteousness and obeyed the law for us.
	+ His holy life was a perfect commentary on the law of God.
* **Passive Obedience**
	+ Christ suffered the penalty due to us for our guilt.
	+ He appeared to put away sin by the sacrifice of himself.
* **Why a Priest was Needed**
	+ A priest was needed to mediate between a guilty creature and a holy God.
	+ Christ was the mediator between God and humanity.
* **Christ's Suffering**
	+ Christ suffered only in his human nature.
	+ His human nature was united to his divine nature, which supported his sufferings.
* **The Greatness of Christ's Sufferings**
	+ Christ's sufferings were truly great, not just in appearance.
	+ He suffered an ignominious, painful, and cursed death on the cross.

• **Christ's Sufferings**

	+ We should pursue sin with holy malice and shed the blood of those sins that shed Christ's blood

	+ He never dies (Heb 7:23-24)

	+ Only for the elect (John 17:9)
	+ For the weakest believers (John 17:20)
	+ For all the sins of believers (John 17:20)

	+ Presents the merit of his blood to his Father (Lev 16:11-16)
	+ Pleads for mercy in the virtue of that price paid (Lev 16:11-16)
	+ Spreads his blood before his Father (Lev 16:11-16)

**II. Christ's Intercession in Action**

* **Christ's Intercession is a Melodious Sound**
	+ His intercession makes a sound in the ears of God (Psalm 2:11)
	+ He is mindful of his mystic body (Heb 2:17)
* **Christ's Intercession is Effective**
	+ He is true to the cause he pleads (Heb 2:17)
	+ He does not forget any cause he has to plead (Heb 2:17)
* **Christ's Intercession is Unique**
	+ He has no succession in his priesthood (Heb 7:23-24)
	+ He is the great Master of requests in heaven (Rom 8:34)

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Why was Christ made of a woman?**
	+ To fulfill the promise in Genesis 3:15, "The seed of the woman shall break the serpent's head."
	+ To roll away the reproach from the woman, who had been seduced by the serpent.

• **Why was Christ born of a woman?**
	+ To honor her gender and make amends for her role in making man a sinner.

• **Why was Christ born of a virgin?**
	+ For decency, as it was not fitting for God to have a mother who was not a virgin.
	+ For necessity, as Christ needed to be a high priest who was pure and holy.
	+ To answer the type of Melchisedec, who was said to be "without father and without mother."

• **How could Christ be made of the flesh and blood of a virgin and yet be without sin?**
	+ The Holy Spirit consecrated and purified the part of the virgin's flesh where Christ was made.
	+ The Holy Spirit refined and clarified that part of the virgin's flesh, separating it from sin.

• **What is meant by the power of the Most High overshadowing the virgin?**
	+ The Holy Spirit united Christ's human nature to his divine nature, making one person.

• **When was Christ incarnate?**
	+ In the fullness of time, when all the prophecies of the coming of the Messiah were accomplished.

• **Why was it necessary for Christ to be incarnate?**
	+ Because only a man could satisfy God's justice, and only God could do it.

• **Why was it necessary for Christ to be both God and man?**
	+ Because only a being who was both God and man could undertake the work of redemption.

• **What is the significance of Christ being made of a woman?**
	+ It shows that God's plan of salvation is not just for men, but for all people.

• **What is the significance of Christ being born of a virgin?**
	+ It shows that Christ's birth was a unique and special event, and that he was set apart for a holy purpose.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Why was Jesus Christ made flesh?**
	+ It was due to free grace and love in God the Father and Christ.
	+ Christ's love for humanity motivated him to take on human form.
	+ Christ's taking flesh was a plot of free grace and a design of pure love.

• **Christ took our flesh to take our sins upon him**
	+ He bore the weight of the sins of the whole world.
	+ He took our flesh to appease God's wrath for us.

• **Christ's incarnation made human nature lovely to God**
	+ Our fallen nature was odious to God, but Christ's taking flesh made it lovely.
	+ Christ's human nature shines and appears amiable in God's eyes.

• **Christ's incarnation made divine nature lovely to man**
	+ The pure Godhead is terrible to behold, but Christ's human nature makes it more amiable.
	+ We can now look upon God through Christ's human nature without fear.

• **Christ united himself to man to draw us near to God**
	+ Christ mediates for us and brings us into favor with God.
	+ He makes peace between us and God, the Father.

• **Christ's incarnation is a monument of free grace**
	+ It demonstrates God's love and wisdom.
	+ It shows that God is a lover of humanity.

• **Christ's love for humanity motivated his incarnation**
	+ He came out of pity and love for us.
	+ Our misery, not our deserts, motivated Christ to take flesh.

• **Christ's incarnation is a master-piece of wisdom**
	+ It shows God's wisdom in creating a plan to redeem humanity.
	+ It demonstrates God's ability to overcome human sin and weakness.

• **Christ's incarnation makes us friends with God again**
	+ He brings us into favor with God through his mediation.
	+ He makes peace between us and God, the Father.

• **Christ's incarnation is a demonstration of God's love**
	+ It shows God's love for humanity in sending his Son to redeem us.
	+ It demonstrates God's willingness to forgive and redeem humanity.

* **The Incarnation of Christ**

	+ He humbled himself more in lying in the virgin's womb than in hanging on the cross

	+ Christ's flesh is called a veil, which veiled his glory
	+ For him to be made flesh, who was equal with God, is a remarkable act of humility

	+ Christ's love should affect our hearts and move us to love him in return
	+ His love is a mystery that is beyond human understanding, but it is real and powerful.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Christ's Humility**
	+ He took on human form, equal with God, yet humbled himself
	+ He stripped himself of divine glory and took on human weakness
	+ He took on the worst of human nature, under disgrace

• **Christ's Infirmites**
	+ He took on painful infirmities, such as:
		- Hunger (Matt 21:18, 19)
		- Weariness (John 4:6)
		- Sorrow (Matt 26:38)
		- Fear (Heb 5:7)
	+ He did not take on sinful infirmities, such as covetousness or ambition

• **Christ's Sinful Flesh**
	+ He was made in the likeness of sinful flesh (2 Cor 5:21)
	+ He was numbered among transgressors (Isa 53:12)
	+ He bore the sins of many (Heb 9:28)

• **The Lowest Degree of Humiliation**
	+ Christ being reputed as a sinner was the greatest pattern of humility
	+ He endured to have sin imputed to him, despite being sinless

• **Lessons from Christ's Humility**
	+ Christians should not be proud of their fine feathers or accomplishments
	+ They should be humble, recognizing that all they have is from God

• **The Dangers of Pride**
	+ God hates the semblance of pride (Proverbs 8:13)
	+ Pride can lead to destruction, as seen in the angels who were spared not (Augustine)

• **The Importance of Humility**
	+ It is better to lack abilities than to lack humility
	+ Humility is a key characteristic of a Christian

• **The Paradox of God in Human Form**
	+ That God should be made in man's image is a greater wonder than man being made in God's image
	+ The Ancient of Days being born, crying in the cradle, and sucking the breast is a sacred riddle or paradox

• **The Significance of Christ's Humiliation**
	+ It shows the depth of God's love for humanity
	+ It demonstrates the extent of Christ's sacrifice for our sins

• **The Call to Humility**
	+ Christians should look to Christ as a pattern of humility
	+ They should be humbled, recognizing their own sinfulness and dependence on God

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Miracle of Christ's Birth**
	+ Christ was born of a woman, and that woman was a virgin
	+ This is a miracle that we may never fully understand until we reach heaven
	+ It is a demonstration of God's power and love

• **God's Ability to Do the Impossible**
	+ God can bring about things that are impossible in nature
	+ Examples include iron swimming, rocks gushing out water, and fire consuming water
	+ God's power is not limited by human reason or understanding

• **The Impossibility of God's Union with Human Flesh**
	+ It is impossible for us to understand how God can be united with human flesh
	+ God can do what transcends reason and exceeds faith

	+ We may question how God can bring about success in such situations
	+ We need to remember that God's power is not limited by human understanding

• **Examples of Discouragement**
	+ Moses was discouraged when faced with the task of feeding 600,000 people for a month
	+ He questioned how this could be done, even with all the flocks and herds in the land
	+ God reassured Moses that there was no limit to His power

• **God's Power to Do the Impossible**
	+ God can bring about things that seem impossible to us
	+ He can subdue a proud heart and raise a dying church
	+ He can do what seems impossible to us, including bringing Christ into the world

• **The Importance of Christ Being Born in Our Hearts**
	+ Christ's birth in our hearts is just as important as his birth in the world
	+ We need to labor to make Christ spiritually born in our hearts
	+ This involves experiencing spiritual pangs and deep convictions

• **Signs of Christ Being Born in Our Hearts**
	+ We experience pangs of conscience and deep convictions
	+ We are deeply afflicted for sin and recognize our need for Christ
	+ We are not yet at peace with God if we have not experienced these pangs

	+ Faith is necessary to understand God's power and ability to do the impossible
	+ We need to trust in God's power and love, even when things seem impossible
	+ Faith is the key to understanding God's ways and experiencing His power

	+ We need to rest on the arm of God's power and trust in His ability to do the impossible
	+ We should not be discouraged by seeming impossibilities, but rather trust in God's power to bring about success
	+ God's power is not limited by human understanding or reason.

• **Christ's Birth in Our Hearts**

	+ **Disposition**: be like Christ in sweetness of disposition, pitying and loving others
	+ **Humility**: be like Christ in humility, humbling ourselves and leaving our pride behind
	+ **Other aspects**: (not specified in the original text, but implied to be part of the five things)

• **Being Like Christ in Disposition**
	+ Be like Christ in mildness and sweetness, not being ill-tempered or violent

	+ Be like Christ in having his grace, which is a wonder of humility

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Glory of Humility**
	+ Humility is a despising of self-excellence, a kind of self-annihilation

	+ Our sins are represented to us in the looking-glass of conscience

	+ We may see other Christians outshining us in gifts and graces
	+ Others may be laden with fruit, while we have only a few olive berries
	+ This can humble us and remind us of our own limitations

	+ We came from the earth, which is the most ignoble element
	+ We are but walking dirt, the son of dust, and the son of nothing
	+ This should humble us and remind us of our mortality

• **Looking Above Us**
	+ We see God resisting the proud and pursuing them in vengeance
	+ The proud are like a mark that God shoots at and never misses
	+ This should humble us and remind us of God's power and judgment

	+ Christ came not in the pride of flesh, but was of lowly parentage
	+ He declined worldly dignity and greatness, and chose to ride on a donkey and hang on a cross
	+ We should be like Christ in heavenliness and contempt of the world

	+ Zeal is necessary for our duties to be acceptable to God
	+ Without zeal, our duties are like a lute without bow-strings, making no music
	+ Zeal without prudence is rashness, and prudence without zeal is cowardliness

• **The Dangers of Worldly Ambition**
	+ We should not be ambitious of the empty honors and glories of the world
	+ We should not purchase the world with the loss of our soul
	+ A wise man would not damn himself to grow rich or throw down his soul to hell to build up an earthly estate

	+ We should be like Christ in humility, zeal, and contempt of the world
	+ We should imitate Christ's example and follow his teachings
	+ By doing so, we will be lovely in God's eyes and experience the glory of humility.

	+ Christ took our flesh and was humbled for us
	+ We should be willing to be humbled and abased for Christ
	+ "If to serve my Lord Christ, if to keep my conscience pure—if this is to be vile—I will yet be more vile!" (David)

	+ Christ's taking of our flesh has ennobled our nature
	+ Our nature is now invested with greater royalties and privileges

	+ God exalted Christ to the highest place and gave him the name that is above every name (Phil 2:9)
	+ Christ's exaltation consists of his rising from the dead, ascending into heaven, and sitting at the right hand of God

• **Ways in Which Christ is Exalted**
	+ 1. In his titles: Christ is exalted to be a Lord
	+ 2. In his office: Christ has all power and authority
	+ 3. In his ascension: Christ rose from the dead and ascended into heaven
	+ 4. To his right-hand: Christ sits at the right hand of God
	+ 5. In constituting him judge of the world: Christ will judge the world

	+ Christ is a Lord in respect of his sovereignty
	+ He is Lord over angels and men
	+ "All power is given to him" (Matthew 28:18)

• **Christ's Ascension and Right-Hand Position**
	+ Christ rose from the dead and ascended into heaven
	+ Christ sits at the right hand of God, a position of power and authority

• **Christ's Office and Authority**
	+ Christ has all power and authority
	+ He is the judge of the world and will render judgment on all people

• **Christ's Exaltation and Our Reward**
	+ Those who suffer for Christ's sake will be rewarded
	+ "Those who take away a saint's reputation, shall add to his reward." (Augustine)

• **Christ's Glory and Our Glory**
	+ The same glory that is put upon Christ's human nature will be put upon believers
	+ We are made one with God and are his members (Eph 5:30, 1:23)

• **Christ's Power and Authority**
	+ He holds the keys of death and the grave (Revelation 1:18)
	+ He has the power to lock up the damned in hell
	+ He is the living one who died and is alive forever (Revelation 1:18)

	+ He is exalted to be a Prince (Psalm 2:12)
	+ He is the great Prince who stands watch over God's people (Daniel 12:1)
	+ He is the Prince of the kings of the earth

	+ He is honored to be the Savior of the world (Acts 4:12)
	+ He saves from sin (Matthew 1:21) and from wrath (1 Thessalonians 1:10)
	+ He is the only one who can save (Acts 4:12)

• **Christ's Ascension**
	+ He ascended into heaven (Luke 24:51, Ephesians 4:10)
	+ He is far above all heavens (Ephesians 4:10)
	+ He is ascended into the highest part of the empyrean heaven (2 Corinthians 12:2)

• **The Manner of Christ's Ascension**
	+ He blessed his disciples before ascending (Luke 24:50-51)
	+ He left them his blessing, not his earthly possessions
	+ He ascended as a conqueror, triumphing over sin, hell, and death

• **The Fruit of Christ's Ascension**
	+ His ascension causes the descension of the Holy Spirit into our hearts
	+ He gives gifts to men, including the gift of his Spirit (Ephesians 4:8)

• **Christ's Exaltation to God's Right Hand**
	+ He was received up into heaven and sat upon the right hand of God (Mark 16:19)
	+ He is seated at God's right hand in the heavenly realms (Ephesians 1:20-21)

• **The Meaning of Christ Sitting at God's Right Hand**
	+ It's a metaphor taken from the manner of kings, who advance their favorites next to their own persons
	+ It signifies Christ's exaltation and authority over all rule and authority

• **Christ's Exaltation and Our Response**
	+ We must bow to Christ as Lord and Judge (Psalm 2:12)
	+ We must kiss Christ with a kiss of love and loyalty (Psalm 2:12)
	+ We must cast ourselves at his feet to serve him

• **The Significance of Christ's Exaltation**
	+ It's a great honor to Christ, worthy of praise and worship
	+ It's a source of joy and celebration for the saints in heaven (Revelation 5:9)

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Christ's Exaltation**
	+ Christ is seated at the right hand of God, a position of dignity and honor.
	+ His human nature is united with the divine nature, making him a mediator filled with majesty and honor.
* **Glory and Honor**
	+ Christ's human nature is glorified, but not deified, through his union with the divine nature.
	+ He is exalted above the highest heavens, far above all heavens.
* **Judgment and Authority**
	+ God has exalted Christ as the Judge of the whole world.

	+ Christ's states are compared: from being on earth to being in heaven.

	+ Humility is the way to true honor, as seen in Christ's example.
	+ Those who humble themselves will be exalted, and will have a greater degree of glory in heaven.
* **Suffering and Glory**
	+ Sufferings must come before glory, as seen in Christ's example.
	+ Those who suffer with Christ will reign with him.

	+ There is no way to heaven but through sufferings.
	+ The cross is the way to the crown.
* **Christ's Glory**
	+ Christ is given a name above every name.
	+ He is clad in princes' robes and the kings of the earth cast their crowns before him.
* **Christ's Coronation**
	+ Christ's coronation is compared to his crucifixion.
	+ He is anointed with the oil of gladness and has been set at God's right hand.
* **The Highest Court**
	+ Christ's judgment is the highest court of judicature, from which there is no appeal.
	+ He will come in the glory of his Father, with all his holy angels.

• **Christ's Exaltation**

	+ As the head is exalted, the body will be exalted also
