Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Exalting Christ**
	+ Exalt Christ's person in our hearts by believing, adoring, and loving him
	+ Exalt Christ's person in our lips by praising and commending him
	+ Exalt Christ's person in our lives by living holy lives
* **Exalting Christ's Truths**
	+ Maintain the truths of Christ against error
	+ Maintain the doctrine of free grace against merit
	+ Maintain the Deity of Christ against Socinianism
* **Christ as Redeemer**
	+ Christ is the glorious purchaser of our redemption
	+ The doctrine of redemption by Jesus Christ is a glorious doctrine
	+ Christ's redemption is greater than creation, as it cost more to redeem us than to make us
* **How Christ Redeems Us**
	+ Christ redeems us by his own precious blood
	+ Christ's blood is a price sufficient for the ransom of millions
	+ Christ's blood is the blood of God as well as man
* **What Christ Redeems Us From**
	+ Christ redeems us from sin
	+ To be redeemed from slavery is a great mercy, but it is infinitely more to be redeemed from sin
	+ Christ's redemption is a work of his arm, not just a word
* **The Spirit's Role in Redemption**
	+ The Spirit applies the redemption purchased by Christ to us
	+ The Spirit works faith in us, uniting us to Christ in our effectual calling
* **The Importance of Redemption**
	+ Redemption is the marrow and quintessence of the gospel
	+ All a Christian's comfort lies in the doctrine of redemption
* **Christ's Right to Redeem Us**
	+ Christ has the best right to redeem us, as he is our kinsman
	+ The Hebrew word for Redeemer, Goel, signifies a kinsman
* **The Price of Redemption**
	+ Christ laid down a price equivalent for the ransom of the prisoner
	+ Christ's blood is the price that ransoms us from sin
* **The Result of Redemption**
	+ We are bought with a price, and that price was Christ's own blood
	+ We are redeemed from slavery to sin, and are now free to serve Christ

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Redemption from Sin**
	+ Sin is the only thing that can hurt the soul
	+ Affliction can make the soul better, but sin damns it
	+ Christ redeems us from sin through his sacrifice

• **How Redemption Works**
	+ Redemption is either begun or perfect
	+ Sin cannot stand with perfect redemption, but it can exist with imperfect redemption on earth
	+ Redemption is complete in glory, but not yet on earth

• **What Christ Has Redeemed Us From**
	+ **Guilt**: Christ has redeemed us from the guilt of sin, discharging our debts
	+ **Power and Dominion**: Christ has redeemed us from the power and dominion of sin, but not from its presence
	+ **Curse**: Christ has redeemed us from the curse due to sin, making him a curse for us

• **What Christ Has Redeemed Us To**
	+ **Glorious Inheritance**: Christ has redeemed us to a glorious inheritance in heaven
	+ **Inheritance in Light**: The heavenly inheritance is irradiated with light, making it a state of happiness
	+ **Incorruptible Inheritance**: The heavenly inheritance is incorruptible, not subject to decay or dissolution
	+ **Undefiled Inheritance**: The heavenly inheritance is undefiled, nothing can stain it, and it is compared to precious stones like gold, sapphire, and emerald

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Nature of Heaven**
	+ Heaven is a place of pure and holy spirits
	+ It is free from lust, malice, and other evil desires
	+ The inhabitants of heaven are pure and virgin spirits

• **The Inheritance of the Saints**
	+ The heavenly inheritance is an unfading and eternal gift
	+ It is a beautiful and glorious inheritance that cannot be fully described
	+ The glory of this inheritance is the eternal sight and fruition of God

• **The Redemption of Christ**
	+ Christ has redeemed the saints to an unfading inheritance
	+ This inheritance is purchased through the redemption of Christ's blood
	+ Christ's blood is a sufficient price for all, but only effective for those who believe

• **The Love of Christ**
	+ Christ's love for the elect is demonstrated by his willingness to die for them
	+ Christ's love is written in characters of blood
	+ Christ's love is a matter of astonishment and wonder

• **The Trial of Redemption**
	+ We must test ourselves to see if we are among those redeemed by Christ
	+ This is a necessary question to ask our own souls
	+ We must examine our hearts and lives to see if we are reconciled to God

• **The Marks of Redemption**
	+ Those who are redeemed are reconciled to God
	+ They have a changed heart and a will inclined to God
	+ They approve of God's judgments and are inclined to holiness

• **The Importance of Faith**
	+ Faith is not able to fully comprehend the reward of redemption
	+ We must trust in Christ's blood and his redemption
	+ Faith is the means by which we receive the benefits of Christ's redemption

• **The Sufficiency and Efficiency of Christ's Blood**
	+ Christ's blood is a sufficient price for all, but only effective for those who believe
	+ A plaster may have healing properties, but it only heals if applied to the wound
	+ Christ's blood is only effective for those who apply it to their hearts and lives

• **The Decrees of God**
	+ Christ's redemption is not as large as creation
	+ There is a certain number of people whom Christ has redeemed
	+ We must not assume that Christ's redemption is universal, but rather that it is specific and particular

• **The Call to Self-Examination**
	+ We must examine our own hearts and lives to see if we are among those redeemed by Christ
	+ We must ask ourselves if we are reconciled to God and if we have a changed heart
	+ We must trust in Christ's blood and his redemption, and apply it to our lives.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Nature of Redemption**
	+ Redemption is a gift from God through Christ's sacrifice (Gal 1:4)
	+ Those redeemed by Christ are risen with Christ (Col 3:1)
	+ They use the world but their hearts are set on heaven
* **The Contrast with the World**
	+ Those redeemed by Christ are "dead to the world" (Gal 6:14)
	+ They are not lovers of the world, but rather of God (1 John 2:15)
	+ They are not bound by earthly things, but rather by their faith in Christ
* **Comfort for the Redeemed**
	+ They are blessed and have received the gift of free grace
	+ They have been freed from sin and its power
	+ They have access to glorious privileges in the gospel, including justification, adoption, and glorification
* **Exhortation to the Redeemed**
	+ Long for the time of full and perfect redemption in heaven
	+ Look forward to being freed from the presence of sin and its effects
	+ Be content to wait for this glorious redemption, knowing that God has prepared something greater than we can imagine
* **The Application of Redemption**
	+ Faith is the means by which the Spirit applies redemption to us (Gal 2:20)
	+ There are four kinds of faith:
		- Historical or dogmatic faith (believing the truths of the Word)
		- Temporary faith (lasting for a time, but then vanishing)
		- Miraculous faith (granted to the apostles to work miracles)
		- True justifying faith (a faith of the operation of God, leading to justification)
* **The Importance of Faith**
	+ Faith in Christ is the comfort of the gospel
	+ Faith in Christ is the means by which we live in the body (Gal 2:20)
	+ Faith in Christ is the key to experiencing the redemption purchased by Christ

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **What Justifying Faith is Not**
	+ Not just a bare acknowledgment of Christ as Savior
	+ Can be present in those who do not have a work of grace on their heart
	+ Can be present in those who assent to divine truth but do not live it out

• **What Justifying Faith Is**
	+ Consists of three things:
		- Self-renunciation
		- Reliance on Christ
		- Appropriation of Christ

• **Self-Renunciation**
	+ Involves going out of oneself and recognizing that we have no righteousness of our own
	+ Involves repentance and humility
	+ Recognizes that we are helpless and must find help in another

• **Reliance on Christ**
	+ Involves casting oneself upon Jesus Christ and resting on his person
	+ Involves believing the promise, but resting on Christ's person
	+ Involves glorying in the cross of Christ

• **Appropriation of Christ**
	+ Involves applying Christ to oneself through faith
	+ Involves receiving Christ's golden merits with salvation
	+ Involves enriching oneself through faith

• **How Faith is Wrought**
	+ By the blessed Spirit, who is called the "Spirit of grace"
	+ Through the Spirit's power, which is described as "the exceeding greatness of God's power"
	+ Through the Spirit's work in irradiating the mind and subduing the will

• **The Preciousness of Faith**
	+ Faith is the chief gospel-grace and the head of the graces
	+ Faith gives a title to glory and is the means of possessing it
	+ Faith is the crowning grace in heaven, but for now, it must take precedence over love

• **Faith is Not Just a Feeling**
	+ Faith is not just a feeling or an emotion, but a work of the Spirit
	+ Faith involves a deep understanding of the gospel and a commitment to it
	+ Faith involves a willingness to submit to Christ and be ruled by him

• **Faith is Not Just a Decision**
	+ Faith is not just a decision or a choice, but a deep and abiding trust in Christ
	+ Faith involves a recognition of one's own helplessness and the need for Christ's salvation
	+ Faith involves a commitment to follow Christ and live according to his teachings

• **Faith is the Key to Salvation**
	+ Faith is the means by which we receive salvation and eternal life
	+ Faith involves a deep trust in Christ and a recognition of his power to save
	+ Faith is the key to unlocking the riches of Christ and possessing eternal life.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Faith is the Conquering Grace**
	+ Faith is the victory that overcomes the world (1 John 5:4)
	+ It sets all other graces to work, including hope, love, and patience

• **Faith Influences All Graces**
	+ Faith sets hope to work by believing in the title to heaven's glories
	+ Faith sets love to work by believing in Christ's mercy and merit
	+ Faith sets patience to work by believing in the rewards of suffering

• **Faith is the Master-Wheel**
	+ Faith is the central grace that sets all other graces in motion
	+ It is the foundation upon which all other graces are built

• **Faith is Honored by God**
	+ God honors faith by justifying and saving those who have it
	+ Faith is the precious gift that justifies and saves

• **How Faith Justifies**
	+ Faith justifies by laying hold of Christ's merits, not by any inherent virtue
	+ Faith is like a ring that contains a precious stone, which is the source of healing

• **Why Faith Saves and Justifies**
	+ Because of God's purpose to use faith as the justifying grace
	+ Because faith takes a man off himself and gives all the honor to Christ and free grace

• **Faith Makes Us One with Christ**
	+ Faith gives us coalition and union with Christ's person
	+ It makes us members of Christ, not just like Him

• **The Importance of Faith**
	+ Faith is more useful than any other grace, even if it is weak
	+ Without faith, it is impossible to please God

• **The Trial of Faith**
	+ We must be serious in testing whether we have true faith
	+ Faith that looks like true faith may be distinguished by its fruits

• **The Consequences of Weak Faith**
	+ If our faith is not good, nothing good comes from us
	+ Even our duties and graces are adulterated if our faith is weak

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Faith is a Christ-prizing grace**
	+ It puts a high valuation on Christ
	+ We should be willing to part with earthly things for Christ
	+ Example: Paul's statement "I count all things but dung, that I may win Christ"

• **Faith is a refining grace**
	+ It purifies the heart and makes it a holy of holies
	+ It takes away the love of sin, even if it doesn't take away the life of sin
	+ Example: "Having purified their hearts by faith"

• **Faith is an obediential grace**
	+ It melts our will into God's and runs at God's call
	+ It obeys God's commands, even if they are difficult
	+ Example: "The obedience of faith" and Abraham's obedience in Heb 11:1

• **Faith is an assimilating grace**
	+ It changes the soul into the image of Christ
	+ It makes us like Christ in character and behavior
	+ Example: Looking on Christ transforms us and turns us into his similitude

• **True faith grows**
	+ It grows in strength, enabling us to do more than before
	+ It grows in fervency and love for God
	+ Example: We can perform duties in a more spiritual manner with more love

• **A weak faith is still true faith**
	+ A weak faith is not the same as no faith
	+ A weak faith can still receive a strong Christ
	+ Example: The woman who touched Christ's garment with a weak faith still received virtue from him

• **The promise is made to true faith, not strong faith**
	+ The promise is not limited to those with giant faith
	+ True faith, even if weak, can still receive the promise
	+ Example: The promise is made to those who have faith, not to those who have a certain level of faith

• **Faith is not just knowledge**
	+ The devil has knowledge, but lacks obedience
	+ True faith is evidenced by obedience, not just knowledge
	+ Example: The devil's knowledge is not enough to make him a believer

• **Faith is a heavenly plant**
	+ It will not grow in impure soil
	+ It requires a heart that is pure and holy
	+ Example: Faith is a virgin-grace that takes away the love of sin

• **Faith is a cheerful obedience**
	+ True obedience is a cheerful obedience
	+ We should look upon God's commands as a privilege, not a burden
	+ Example: God's commands do not seem grievous to those who have faith.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Faith and Salvation**
	+ The promise of salvation is made to whoever believes, regardless of the size of their faith.
	+ A weak faith may still be fruitful and lead to strong affections.
	+ God will not refuse a weak believer, and they are still a member of Christ.

• **Effectual Calling**
	+ Effectual calling is a gracious work of the Spirit that causes us to embrace Christ freely.
	+ It is a new creation, the first resurrection, and a two-fold call: outward and inward.

• **Outward Call**
	+ The outward call is God's offer of grace to sinners, inviting them to accept Christ and salvation.
	+ It shows men what they must do to be saved and renders them inexcusable in case of disobedience.

• **Inward Call**
	+ The inward call is when God works grace in the heart and draws the will to embrace Christ.
	+ It leads to a possession of Christ, not just a profession.

• **Means of Effectual Call**
	+ Every creature has a voice to call us, but only God's voice converts.
	+ The two means of effectual call are:
		- The preaching of the word (God speaks through his ministers).
		- The Holy Spirit (the ministry of the word is the pipe or organ, and the Spirit is the one who blows and changes hearts).

• **God's Call**
	+ God calls men from sin, ignorance, and unbelief.
	+ He calls them from darkness to light, from the fire and brimstone of hell, and from all curses to which they were exposed.

• **God's Call to the World**
	+ God calls men out of the world, as Christ called Matthew from his collection of taxes.
	+ They are not of the world, and God's call is a call to leave the world behind.

• **The Voice of God**
	+ God speaks to us through his ministers, and in every sermon preached, God calls to us.
	+ To refuse the message we bring is to refuse God himself.

• **The Role of the Holy Spirit**
	+ The Holy Spirit is the one who blows and changes hearts, making the ministry of the word effective.
	+ The Spirit comes with a key and opens the door to the heart.

• **The Nature of Effectual Calling**
	+ It is a gracious work of the Spirit that causes us to embrace Christ freely.
	+ It is a new creation, the first resurrection, and a two-fold call: outward and inward.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Who are called by God?**
	+ They are not natives of this world, but pilgrims
	+ They do not conform to the world or follow its sinful fashions
	+ They are not of the world, but trade in the heavenly country

• **What does God call men to?**
	+ Holiness (1 Thess 4:7)
	+ Glory (2 Cor 4:17)
	+ A weight of glory that is permanent and eternal

• **What is the cause of the effectual call?**
	+ God's electing love (Romans 8:30)
	+ Election is the fountain-cause of our effectual calling
	+ It is not based on merit, but on God's sovereign choice

• **What are the characteristics of the effectual call?**
	+ **Powerful**: God puts forth infinite power in calling home a sinner to himself
	+ **High**: It is a call to a weight of glory that is permanent and eternal

• **What is the nature of the effectual call?**
	+ It is not a moral persuasion, but a powerful and enabling call
	+ It is not just a proposal of promises and threatenings, but a new creation

• **How does the effectual call compare to Satan's power?**
	+ God's power in converting is greater than Satan's power in seducing
	+ The effectual call is a kind of omnipotence that enables men to resist sin

• **What is the relationship between election and the effectual call?**
	+ Election is the foundation of the effectual call
	+ It is not based on merit, but on God's sovereign choice

• **What is the nature of the called?**
	+ They are anointed with the consecrating oil of the Spirit
	+ They are the people of God's holiness

• **What is the purpose of the effectual call?**
	+ To bring men to holiness and glory
	+ To enable men to resist sin and follow God

• **What is the result of the effectual call?**
	+ A new creation (Ezek 36:27)
	+ A weight of glory that is permanent and eternal

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The High Calling of God**
	+ It is a high calling because we are called to high exercises of piety
	+ We are called to live by faith, love God, and be living organs of his praise
	+ We are called to hold communion with the Father and the Son

• **High Privileges**
	+ We are called to justification and adoption
	+ We are called to be kings and priests unto God
	+ We are called to be co-heirs with Christ

• **Immutability of the Call**
	+ God's gracious gifts and calling are irrevocable
	+ Those gifts that flow from election (such as effectual calling and justification) are irrevocable
	+ God never repents of calling a sinner to be a saint

• **Necessity of the Effectual Call**
	+ A man cannot go to heaven without it
	+ We must be called before we are glorified
	+ A man uncalled can lay claim to nothing in the Bible but threatening

• **Trial of the Effectual Call**
	+ We may know we are effectually called by its antecedent (conviction of sin) and its results
	+ Conviction of sin is the first step in conversion from sin
	+ He who is savingly called answers to God's call and stops his ears to all other calls

• **Results of the Effectual Call**
	+ He who is savingly called answers to God's call
	+ He who is effectually called stops his ears to all other calls which would call him off from God

• **Comfort to Those Who Are Called**
	+ The effectual call evidences election
	+ Election is the cause of our effectually calling, and effectually calling is the sign of our election
	+ Effectually calling is the pledge of glory

• **Thankfulness for the Effectual Call**
	+ We should be thankful to God for the unspeakable blessing of the effectual call
	+ We should be thankful to the Father's mercy, the Son's merit, and the Spirit's efficacy
	+ We should consider what we were before God called us

• **Characteristics of the Effectual Call**
	+ It is a call that convinces us of sin
	+ It is a call that makes us fit to receive the seeds of grace
	+ It is a call that we answer to and obey

• **Consequences of the Effectual Call**
	+ We are called to be co-heirs with Christ
	+ We are called to be kings and priests unto God
	+ We are called to be princes in all lands, though princes in disguise

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **God's Sovereign Love**
	+ God calls people in their sin, not in their righteousness
	+ He passes by wise and noble people to call the most unlikely
	+ This is a demonstration of His astonishing love and mercy

• **The Favor of Being Called**
	+ It's a great favor to be called by God, as seen in the stories of Samuel and Zacchaeus
	+ God's call is not based on our worthiness, but on His sovereign will

• **The Banner of Free Grace**
	+ God's call is a display of His free and sovereign grace
	+ It's a call to praise and thanksgiving, as seen in the story of Elijah and Elisha

• **The Call to Praise**
	+ Those who are called by God should be affected with His sovereign love
	+ They should run over with thankfulness and stand on Mount Gerizim, blessing and praising God

• **The Call to Walk Worthy**
	+ Those who are called by God should walk worthy of their high calling
	+ This involves living a life of compassion and holiness

• **Walking Compassionately**
	+ We should pity those who are yet uncalled and under the power of Satan
	+ We should weep over their dying souls and pray for their salvation

• **Walking Holily**
	+ Our calling is a holy calling, and we should live a holy life
	+ We should show our effectual calling by living a Bible life and avoiding sin

• **The Dignity of Our Calling**
	+ We are called out of the world and have the mark of election upon us
	+ We should remember our dignity and live in a way that honors our high calling

• **Separation from Sin**
	+ As those who are called by God, we should abhor all motions to sin
	+ We should live in a way that is separate from the world and its sinful ways

• **The Consecration of Our Bodies**
	+ Our bodies are the temple of the Holy Spirit, and we should live in a way that honors this consecration
	+ We should avoid sin and live a life that is pleasing to God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text on justification:

• **What is Justification?**
	+ An act of God's free grace
	+ Pardons all sins and accepts us as righteous in his sight
	+ Based on the righteousness of Christ, imputed to us and received by faith alone

• **Source of Justification**
	+ The free grace of God
	+ Not based on our worthiness, but God's love and favor
	+ A mercy spun out of the affections of free grace

• **Ground of Justification**
	+ Christ's satisfaction made to his Father
	+ God's justice and holiness are satisfied through Christ's death and merits

• **Method of Justification**
	+ Imputation of Christ's righteousness to us
	+ This righteousness is a better righteousness than the angels'

• **Means of Justification**
	+ Faith
	+ Faith lays hold on Christ's merits and makes us righteous

• **Efficient Cause of Justification**
	+ The whole Trinity (Father, Son, and Holy Spirit)
	+ Each person in the Trinity has a hand in the justification of a sinner

• **What is Meant by Justification?**
	+ A declaration of righteousness, as if we had not sinned
	+ A pronouncement of innocence, based on Christ's satisfaction

• **How is Christ's Satisfaction Meritorious?**
	+ In respect of the divine nature, Christ satisfied God's justice
	+ Christ's death and merits are sufficient to justify us

• **End of Justification**
	+ To the praise of the glory of God's grace
	+ To raise everlasting trophies of God's honor and praise

• **Role of Faith in Justification**
	+ Faith is the instrument of our justification
	+ Faith lays hold on Christ's merits and makes us righteous

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Justification is a real benefit**
	+ It brings dignity and glory to the justified person
	+ It is a real change in the person's status, not just a feeling or perception

• **Justification is not from eternity**
	+ We are born under a sentence of condemnation (John 3:18)
	+ Justification is only for those who believe and repent (Acts 3:19)

• **Justification is not based on repentance alone**
	+ While repentance is necessary, it is not the sole basis for justification
	+ God justifies us, not because of our repentance, but because of His own righteousness

• **Justification is a fixed and permanent thing**
	+ It cannot be lost, even if a person falls from degrees of grace
	+ If a person is justified, they are elected and cannot fall from their justification

• **Justification is not based on our own righteousness**
	+ We have no righteousness within us that could justify us
	+ Justification is based on righteousness imputed to us from outside of ourselves

• **All believers are alike justified**
	+ Justification does not apply to some more than to others
	+ One believer is as perfectly justified as another, regardless of their faith or stature in Christ

• **Justification is accompanied by sanctification**
	+ Whoever God justifies, He also sanctifies (1 Cor. 6:11)
	+ Holiness is the attendant of justification, not the cause

• **Justification is not the same as justification by works**
	+ The apostle Paul says that justification is not based on works (Romans 3:28)
	+ The Papists' doctrine of justification by works is incorrect

• **Justification is not a license for sin**
	+ The Antinomians' doctrine of justification from eternity leads to licentiousness
	+ Justification is not a justification of sin, but a justification of the sinner

• **Justification is a key to glorification**
	+ Justification is the first step towards glorification (Romans 8:30)
	+ Those who are justified will also be glorified, as Joseph was made Lord of the kingdom after being loosed from prison.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Justification by faith, not works**
	+ The Bible teaches that we are justified by faith, not by our good works.
	+ Abraham, a regenerate man, was justified by faith, not by his works.
* **Works are evidences of justification, not causes**
	+ Good works are a result of justification, not the cause of it.
	+ Works are like a handmaid, following justification, not an usher that precedes it.
* **The apostle James' statement explained**
	+ James says Abraham was justified by works, but this means works manifest our righteousness to men, not to God.
	+ Works are evidences of our justification, not the cause of it.
* **The importance of justification**
	+ Justification is necessary for glorification (Rom 8:30).
	+ We must be justified before God to be restored to our former rights and liberties.
* **The utility and benefit of justification**
	+ Justification brings peace with God (Rom 5:1).
	+ Peace is a richer jewel than any earthly treasure.
* **The wonder of justification by God's free grace**
	+ God's way of justifying us by rich grace and precious blood is a mystery that fills us with wonder and love.
	+ The angels admire this mystery, and we should too.
* **Labor for justification**
	+ Christ offers himself and his merits to justify us.
	+ We should labor for this high privilege of justification.
* **The remedy for sinners**
	+ Sinners should not neglect justification offered freely by Christ in the gospel.
	+ We should look after justification through Christ's blood.
* **The love of Christ should not be slighted**
	+ The love of Christ is worth more than our souls and heaven.
	+ We should not neglect justification offered to us freely.
* **The necessity of being justified**
	+ We must be justified before God to be restored to our former rights and liberties.
	+ Justification is necessary for us to enjoy peace with God and have a right to happiness.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Comfort in case of failings**
	+ Believers should not be humbled by their defects, but not despond
	+ They are justified by the righteousness of Christ, not their duties or graces

• **Comfort in case of hard censures**
	+ The world may censure the godly, but God has justified them
	+ God will openly justify them at the day of judgment and pronounce them righteous

• **Adoption**
	+ The qualification for adoption is receiving Christ, which is the same as believing in Him

• **Types of sonship**
	+ Our sonship differs from Christ's, who was the Son of God by eternal generation
	+ Our sonship is:
		- By creation (no privilege, as anyone can have God as their Father)
		- By adoption (a privilege, as it involves a personal relationship with God)

• **Adoption is twofold**
	+ External and federal: those who live in a visible church and make a profession of God are considered sons
	+ Real and gracious: those who are God's favorites and are heirs of glory are considered sons

• **Adoption takes in all nations**
	+ The charter of adoption has been enlarged to include believing Gentiles
	+ All nations can have a right to the privileges of adoption

• **Adoption takes in both sexes**
	+ Females are as capable of adoption as males
	+ God is a father to both sons and daughters

• **Adoption is an act of pure grace**
	+ It is a mercy spun out of the affections of free grace
	+ God is pleased to adopt one and not another, making one a vessel of glory and another a vessel of wrath

• **The nature of adoption**
	+ It is taking a stranger into the relation of a son and heir
	+ It involves a personal relationship with God, where the adopted heir is considered a child of God

• **The privilege of adoption**
	+ It involves dignity and prerogative, as the adopted heir is considered a child of God
	+ It involves an interest in God as a father, where the adopted heir can cry out to God and expect a response.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **What God does in adoption**
	+ Ennobles us with his name
	+ Consecrates us with his Spirit
	+ Gives us a new nature

• **From what state does God adopt us?**
	+ From a state of sin and misery
	+ From slavery (Ezek 16:6)

• **To what does God adopt us?**
	+ To a state of excellence
	+ To a state of liberty (free from sin, Satan, and the law)
	+ To a state of dignity (heirs of promise, honorable, and precious)

• **What is the result of adoption?**
	+ We become God's treasure and jewels
	+ We have angels as our life-guards
	+ We are of the royal blood of heaven

• **What is the glorious inheritance of adoption?**
	+ A kingdom that excels all earthly monarchies
	+ Riches (gates of pearl, streets of pure gold)
	+ Tranquility (peace, no divisions or invasions)
	+ Stability (a kingdom that cannot be shaken)

• **What is the organic or instrumental cause of adoption?**
	+ Faith (we are children of God by faith in Christ Jesus)
	+ Faith is the means by which we are interested in the privilege of adoption

• **What is the effect of adoption on our worship?**
	+ We are free to worship God in the manner we choose
	+ We have God's free Spirit, which makes us cheerful in service

• **What is the significance of adoption?**
	+ It is a mercy to redeem a slave, but it is more to adopt him
	+ Adoption is a state of freedom from the dominion of sin

• **What is the ultimate goal of adoption?**
	+ Coronation (adoption ends in coronation)
	+ We are made heirs of heaven and reign forever and ever

• **What is the nature of the kingdom we inherit?**
	+ It is a kingdom that cannot be shaken
	+ It is a kingdom of peace, with no divisions or invasions

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Love of God in Adoption**
	+ God's love is shown in making us his sons
	+ This is a greater love than making us men or philosophers
	+ It is a wonder of God's love to adopt us as his sons

• **Why Faith is the Instrument of Adoption**
	+ Faith is a quickening grace that makes us alive
	+ It makes us Christ's brethren and God our Father
	+ Faith is the reason we are called children of God

• **The Six Wonders of God's Love in Adoption**
	+ **1. Adopting us when he had a Son of his own**
		- God had a worthy Son, Jesus, and yet adopted us
		- This is a wonder of love that God would adopt us
	+ **2. Adopting us in our deformed state**
		- We were polluted and black as Ethiopians, but God adopted us
		- God's love is shown in adopting us in our sinful state

• **The Expense of God's Love in Adoption**
	+ God's adoption of us was purchased at a great cost
	+ The blood of Jesus was shed to seal the deed of adoption
	+ This is a wonder of God's love that he would go to such expense

• **God's Love in Adopting His Enemies**
	+ God adopted us when we were his enemies
	+ This is a greater love than pardoning our sins
	+ It is a wonder of God's love that he would adopt us as his sons

• **God's Love in Adopting Many**
	+ God brings many sons to glory through Christ
	+ This is a wonder of God's love that he would adopt so many
	+ It opens a door of hope for us that we too can be adopted

• **The Honor of Being Adopted by God**
	+ To be the sons of the high God is a great honor
	+ David thought it no small honor to be a king's son-in-law
	+ But to be the sons of God is a greater honor still

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Condition of Unbelievers**
	+ They are not the sons of God
	+ They do not know God
	+ They are "dead in trespasses and sins"
	+ They have no right to inherit the glories of heaven
* **The Two Classes of People**
	+ The sons of God
	+ The heirs of hell
* **The Sign of Adoption**
	+ Obedience to God's commands
* **The Nature of Obedience**
	+ Must be done by a right rule (the Word of God)
		- The touchstone of obedience is the Word of God
	+ Must be done from a right principle (faith)
		- Obedience must come from a good root (faith)
	+ Must be done to a right end (glorifying God)
		- The end of obedience is to glorify God
* **The Importance of Faith**
	+ Faith is the noble principle of obedience
	+ All acceptable works proceed from faith
* **The Difference between Obedience and Idolatry**
	+ Obedience must be consistent with God's revealed will
	+ Idolatry is offering up strange fire (obedience without the Word of God)
* **The Value of Obedience**
	+ Obedience is a sign of adoption
	+ Obedience must be regular and consistent
* **The Danger of Wrong Motives**
	+ The end determines the value of the deed
	+ Doing good works for the wrong reasons can spoil them
* **The Call to Self-Examination**
	+ Let us test ourselves to see if we are the adopted sons and daughters of God
	+ We must examine our hearts and motives to see if we are truly obeying God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **True Obedience**
	+ Should shine, not blaze (Matthew 6:1-2)
	+ Should be motivated by love, not just external actions
	+ Should be uniform, obeying all of God's commands with equal zeal

• **Characteristics of True Obedience**
	+ Uniform: obeying all of God's commands with equal zeal
	+ Constant: abiding in obedience, not fitful or sporadic
	+ Evangelical: approving of every command, delighting in every command, and desiring to obey every command

• **Who Can Obey God in All Things?**
	+ Adopted heirs of heaven cannot obey every precept perfectly
	+ However, they can obey evangelically, approving of every command and desiring to obey every command

• **Signs of Adoption**
	+ **Love for God's Presence**: loving to be in God's presence, delighting in ordinances and prayer
	+ **Guidance of God's Spirit**: being led by the Spirit of God, not just having life but also being guided in a course of holiness

• **The Importance of God's Spirit**
	+ God's children need the Spirit to lead them, since they are apt to go wrong
	+ The Spirit guides the will, which is imperious and rebels against conscience and understanding

• **The Relationship Between Obedience and Love**
	+ Obedience should be motivated by love, not just external actions
	+ Love for God's presence and guidance is a key aspect of true obedience

• **The Role of Christ's Blood**
	+ Christ's blood supplies defects in our obedience, making it possible for us to obey evangelically

• **The Nature of True Obedience**
	+ True obedience is not just external actions, but a heart that delights in God's commands and desires to obey them

• **The Importance of Uniform Obedience**
	+ Obedience should be uniform, obeying all of God's commands with equal zeal
	+ This is a key aspect of true child-like obedience

• **The Relationship Between Obedience and Adoption**
	+ True obedience is a sign of adoption, demonstrating that we are children of God
	+ Adoption is marked by a love for God's presence, guidance of God's Spirit, and evangelical obedience.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Signs of Adoption**
	+ Being led by the Spirit in virtuous actions
	+ Having an entire love for all God's children, despite their imperfections
	+ Being guided by the Word and the Spirit in harmony
	+ Being able to rejoice in the benefits of adoption

• **Benefits of Adoption**
	+ Having great privileges and freedoms
		- Being privileged from the hurt of everything
		- Being freed from the sting of affliction and the curse of the law

• **Privileges of Adoption**
	+ Being freed from the hurt of everything
	+ Being bettered by affliction, rather than being made worse
	+ Having Jesus Christ draw out the poison of every affliction

• **Interest in Promises**
	+ Being heirs of the promises
	+ Having a right to every promise in the Bible
	+ Being able to say, "This is mine!" to every promise

• **Adoption as a Free Gift**
	+ Being adopted into God's family as a free gift
	+ Having no worth or excellence in ourselves to merit sonship
	+ Having free grace run through the whole privilege of adoption

• **Comparison to Civil Adoption**
	+ Civil adoption requires some worth or excellence in the person being adopted
	+ Our adoption by God requires no worth or excellence in us

• **Mercy of Adoption**

	+ Being adopted is a further sonship by adoption, beyond sonship by creation

	+ Considering the worthlessness of ourselves before adoption
	+ Considering the excellence of God's mercy in adopting us

• **Privileges of Believers**
	+ Being privileged from the hurt of everything
	+ Being freed from the sting of affliction and the curse of the law

• **Heirs of the Promises**
	+ Being heirs of the promises
	+ Having a right to every promise in the Bible
	+ Being able to say, "This is mine!" to every promise

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text on sanctification:

• **What is Sanctification?**
	+ A supernatural thing, divinely infused
	+ An internal thing, lying chiefly in the heart
	+ An extensive thing, spreading into the whole man
	+ An intense and ardent thing
	+ A beautiful thing
	+ An abiding thing
	+ A progressive thing

• **Counterfeits of Sanctification**
	+ Moral virtue (externally good, but not necessarily holy)
	+ Superstitious devotion (externally religious, but not genuinely holy)
	+ Hypocrisy (pretending to be holy, but not truly so)
	+ Restraining grace (forbearing sin, but not hating it)
	+ Common grace (a slight, transient work of the Spirit, not conversion)

• **Necessity of Sanctification**
	+ God has called us to it
	+ No evidencing of justification without it
	+ No title to the new covenant without it
	+ No going to heaven without it
	+ All holy things are defiled without it
	+ No sign of election without it

• **Signs of Sanctification**
	+ Remembering a time when we were unsanctified
	+ Indwelling of the Holy Spirit
	+ Antipathy against sin
	+ Spiritual performance of duties from a principle of love
	+ Holy life
	+ Steadfast resolution

• **Inducements to Sanctification**
	+ It is the will of God that we should be holy
	+ Jesus Christ has died for our sanctification
	+ Sanctification makes us resemble God
	+ Sanctification is what God bears a great love to
	+ Sanctification is what makes us different from the wicked
	+ It is a shame to have the name of a Christian and lack sanctification
	+ Sanctification fits us for heaven

• **Attaining Sanctification**
	+ Be conversant in the Word of God
	+ Get faith in Christ's blood
	+ Breathe after the Spirit
	+ Associate with sanctified people
	+ Pray for sanctification

• **Nature of Sanctification**
	+ A principle of grace savingly wrought
	+ The heart becomes holy and is made after God's own heart
	+ A sanctified person bears God's name and image

• **Privative Part of Sanctification**
	+ Purging out sin, like leaven or leprosy
	+ Taking away the love of sin, not the life of sin

• **Positive Part of Sanctification**
	+ Spiritual refining of the soul
	+ Renewing of the mind and partaking of the divine nature
	+ Adorning with purity, like the priests in the law

• **Importance of Sanctification**
	+ The main thing a Christian should look after
	+ What are the chief inducements to sanctification?

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Sanctification is a supernatural thing**
	+ It is divinely infused by God
	+ It is a flower of the Spirit's planting, as stated in 1 Peter 1:2
* **Sanctification is an internal thing**
	+ It lies chiefly in the heart
	+ It is called "the adorning the hidden man of the heart" in 1 Peter 3:4
* **Sanctification is an extensive thing**
	+ It spreads into the whole man, as stated in 1 Thessalonians 5:23
	+ It affects the mind, will, and affections, turning them towards God
* **Sanctification is an intense and ardent thing**
	+ It burns within the believer, as stated in Romans 12:2
	+ It is a living and active thing, not just a dead form
* **Sanctification is a beautiful thing**
	+ It makes God and angels fall in love with us
	+ It is the most sparkling jewel in the Godhead, as stated in Psalm 110:3
* **Sanctification is an abiding thing**
	+ It is a state that cannot be lost, as stated in 1 John 3:9
	+ It is a blossom of eternity, not just a temporary thing
* **Sanctification is a progressive thing**
	+ It is growing and increasing, like a seed that grows
	+ It can be more or less intense, but it is always present in the believer
* **Sanctification is a result of God's work**
	+ It is a result of God's sanctifying work, as stated in 1 Peter 1:2
	+ It is a gift from God, not something that can be earned
* **Sanctification is a part of the believer's new nature**
	+ It is a part of the "new man" that is created in Christ, as stated in Colossians 3:10
	+ It is a part of the believer's new life in Christ
* **Sanctification is a source of joy and happiness**
	+ It is the quintessence of happiness, as stated in Psalm 16:11
	+ It brings joy and peace to the believer, as stated in Romans 14:17

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Sanctification is progressive**
	+ It is a continuous process of growing in holiness
	+ A Christian is continually adding to their spiritual stature
	+ Sanctification is not a one-time event, but a lifelong process

• **Christ received the Spirit without measure**
	+ Unlike Christians, who receive the Spirit in measure
	+ This means that Christians can still grow in holiness and increase their faith

• **The image of God is drawn imperfectly in us**
	+ We must continually "mend" and "draw" the image of God in us
	+ This requires effort and a willingness to grow in holiness

• **Moral virtue is not sanctification**
	+ Being just, temperate, and having a kind demeanor is good, but not enough
	+ Moral virtue can be achieved by non-Christians, such as Cato, Socrates, and Aristides

• **Moral virtue can be a counterfeit of sanctification**
	+ It can be a mask for unbelief and a secret antipathy against grace
	+ A person with moral virtue may have a "beautiful color" but a "sting" of unbelief

• **Superstitious devotion is not sanctification**
	+ Adorations, images, altars, and holy water are not sanctifying
	+ These practices do not put intrinsic goodness into a person and do not make them better

• **Superstitious devotion can be a counterfeit of sanctification**
	+ It can be a form of legalism and a reliance on external rituals rather than a heart transformed by God

• **Hypocrisy is a counterfeit of sanctification**
	+ Pretending to have holiness when there is none is a vain thing
	+ Hypocrisy can be a self-delusion and a deception of others and oneself

• **Hypocrisy can be a form of self-deception**
	+ A person who pretends to be holy may deceive others, but ultimately deceives themselves

• **A show of sanctification is not enough**
	+ A pretense of holiness without the oil of saving grace is empty and vain
	+ A person with a "lamp of profession" but lacking the "oil of saving grace" will not be comforted at the hour of death.

* **The Consequences of Not Having Sanctification**
	+ We will not be able to see God in his glory

	+ We will be unable to approach God or enter heaven
* **The Call to Sanctification**
	+ God has called us to sanctification, as well as to glory

Here are 6 bullets with 1-2 sub-bullets each summarizing the text:

* **Sharing Assurance**
	+ Share your experience of God's salvation with others to help them fall in love with God's ways.
	+ This can lead to others becoming proselytes to true religion.
* **Improving Assurance through Comforting Others**
	+ Comfort those who lack assurance by being a good Samaritan and pouring wine and oil into their wounds.
	+ The comfortable experience of one Christian can be communicated to another, reviving and strengthening their faith.
* **Walking Heavily**
	+ Scorn earthly things and live above the world, as you have been fed on the grapes and pomegranates of the holy land.
	+ Don't hanker after worldly things when you have assurance of heaven.
* **Cheerful Walking**
	+ Live with a cheerful disposition, as you have been assured of your adoption by God.
	+ Rejoice and be optimistic, even in difficult circumstances, as you have God's bond to assure you of daily bread and his love to assure you of heaven.
* **Longing for a Glorified State**
	+ If you have an assurance of salvation, let it make you long for a glorified state.
	+ Desire a fuller enjoyment of God in heaven, as you have tasted how sweet the Lord is.
* **Keeping Assurance**
	+ Keep your assurance by prayer, asking God to continue his loving-kindness and not take away the special seal of assurance.
	+ Keep assurance by humility, being low in pride and recognizing God's presence in your life.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **Peace**
	+ Refers to the second fruit of sanctification, after assurance
	+ Is a blessing from God, as stated in 1 Peter 1:2

	+ External peace
		- Domestic peace (peace in a family)
		- Political peace (peace in the state)
		- Ecclesiastical peace (peace in the church)

		- Peace above us (peace with God)
		- Peace within us (peace with conscience)

	+ Has the whole Trinity as its author
		- God the Father is the God of peace
		- God the Son is the Prince of peace
		- Peace is a fruit of the Spirit

	+ Is the God of order and peace
	+ Is the author of peace, as stated in Numbers 6:26

	+ Purchased peace by his blood
	+ Made atonement for us through his sacrifice
* **The Holy Spirit's Role in Peace**
	+ Seals up peace to the conscience
	+ Enables conscience to witness to a man who has the real work of grace
* **Who Can Have Peace?**
	+ Only those who are sanctified and have the real work of grace
	+ Those who are unregenerate and wicked have no peace
* **Signs of a False Peace**
	+ A false peace has much confidence in it, but this confidence is a false hope
	+ A false peace separates things that God has joined together (holiness and peace)
* **Consequences of a False Peace**
	+ Leads to a stupified conscience, not a pacified one
	+ Is a result of the devil's peace, which rocks men to sleep in carnal security
* **Distinguishing True and False Peace**
	+ True peace is everlasting, while false peace is temporary and based on ignorance of danger
	+ True peace is based on the knowledge of eternal happiness, while false peace is based on ignorance of danger

	+ The Spirit of God is called the Comforter
	+ He sheds God's love abroad in the heart, whence flows infinite joy and delight

	+ Joy is a real and rational thing

	+ It is a sweet and delightful passion that arises from the feeling of some good

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**1. God's Comfort in Times of Temptation**

* God comforts the bruised reed with joy to confirm a Christian's title to heaven.
* After Satan's fiery darts, God gives the white stone, a balm to heal a tempted soul.
* The oil of gladness is a better balm than any other to heal a tempted soul.

**2. After Spiritual Desertion**

* Desertion is a poisoned arrow that shoots to the heart, making the soul feel abandoned by God.
* The deserted soul cries out, "Has God rejected me forever?"
* God shines upon the soul, giving it some apprehension of his favor and turning the shadow of death into the light of the morning.

**3. At the Hour of Death**

* God sends the Comforter to comfort the dying Christian.
* Even those who have had no joy in their lifetime experience God's favor at the last hour.
* The godly have some foretastes of God's everlasting favor before they die.

**4. Differences between Worldly and Spiritual Joys**

* Spiritual joys help make us better, while worldly joys often make us worse.
* Spiritual joys are like cordial medicine, which not only comforts but purifies the soul.

**5. Characteristics of Spiritual Joys**

* Spiritual joys are inward, heart joys that bring strength to the soul.
* They are not just external pleasures, but a deep sense of joy that comes from within.
* Spiritual joys are like a spring of water that runs underground, unseen by others.

**6. Worldly Joys vs. Spiritual Joys**

* Worldly joys are like a gilded frontispiece, but the rooms within are hung in mourning.
* Spiritual joys are like a house with a strong foundation, where the joy is deep and lasting.

**7. The Effect of Spiritual Joys**

* Spiritual joys make us stronger and more holy.
* They cause an antipathy against sin and infuse strength to live and suffer for Christ.
* The joy of the Lord is our strength.

**8. The Nature of Spiritual Joys**

* Spiritual joys are not just fleeting pleasures, but a deep and lasting sense of joy.
* They are not just external experiences, but a deep sense of connection with God.

**9. The Importance of Spiritual Joys**

* Spiritual joys are essential for our spiritual growth and development.
* They help us to persevere in our faith and to live a holy life.

**10. The Source of Spiritual Joys**

* Spiritual joys come from God, who is the source of all joy and comfort.
* They are a gift from God, given to us to sustain us in times of temptation and desertion.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Perseverance is a fruit of sanctification**
	+ It is the fifth and last fruit of sanctification
	+ It is the ability to persevere in grace

• **The nature of perseverance**
	+ It is not the same as mere profession or external behavior
	+ It is a vital, not artificial, motion

• **Perseverance is not guaranteed for all**
	+ Some may fall away, like Demas or the angels who lost their grace
	+ However, true believers do not fall away actually and lose all their grace

• **Perseverance may fail in degree**
	+ Grace may fail in degree, but not completely
	+ The godly may make a breach upon their sanctification

• **Decay of grace**
	+ Grace may be dying, but not dead
	+ It may be like fire in the embers, though not quenched

• **Two ways decay of grace can occur**
	+ (1) The lively actings of grace may be suspended
	+ (2) Corruption may work instead of grace, leading to enormous sins

• **Examples of decay of grace**
	+ David's pride and lust
	+ The disciples' striving for greatness

• **Perseverance is not the same as absence of sin**
	+ The saints may come to a point where they have but little faith
	+ However, they cannot come to a point where they have no faith

• **Perseverance is not abolished**
	+ Even when grace is drawn low, it is not drawn dry
	+ Even when the wise virgins slumbered, their lamps were not quite gone out

• **The importance of perseverance**
	+ It is a main comfort for Christians
	+ It is essential for all cheerful endeavors and piety

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

**I. Means of Perseverance**

* **1. Ordinances**
	+ Prayer
	+ The Word
	+ Sacraments
	+ These ordinances nourish and preserve grace
* **2. Sacred Influence of the Spirit**
	+ The Spirit carries on grace to perfection
	+ Excites, strengthens, and increases faith
	+ Keeps the lamp of grace burning
* **3. Christ's Daily Intercession**
	+ Christ prays for believers to hold out in faith
	+ "Father, keep those whom you have given me"
	+ Christ's prayer is a guarantee of perseverance

**II. Arguments for Perseverance**

* **1. Truth of God**
	+ God has asserted and promised perseverance
	+ "His seed remains in him"
	+ "I will give unto them eternal life, and they shall never perish!"
* **2. Power of God**
	+ Each Person in the Trinity has a hand in making a believer persevere
	+ God the Father establishes salvation
	+ God the Son confirms salvation
	+ God the Holy Spirit seals salvation

**III. Additional Points**

* **1. The Spirit's Role**
	+ The Spirit is the spring that feeds the river of life (grace)
	+ The Spirit keeps grace in repair
* **2. The Nature of Grace**
	+ Grace is compared to a river of the water of life
	+ This river can never be dried up
* **3. The Importance of Effort**
	+ Believers must use means (ordinances) to persevere
	+ "Watch and pray"
	+ "Unless you remain in the ship, you cannot be saved"

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Argument 1: Power of God**
	+ The Pelagians believed man could overcome temptation by his own power
	+ Augustine argues that perseverance is a gift from God, not something man can achieve on his own
	+ If man had the power to persevere, why wouldn't Judas and Peter have the same ability?

• **Argument 2: Preservation of Israel**
	+ God preserved Israel in the wilderness until they reached Canaan
	+ Similarly, God will preserve his people in a state of grace until they reach their final destination
	+ This preservation is not always miraculous, but rather a spiritual, invisible care

• **Argument 3: God's Electing Love**
	+ Those whom God has elected to glory cannot fall away finally
	+ Every true believer is elected to glory, and therefore cannot fall away
	+ God's decree in election is sure and unalterable

• **Argument 4: Union with Christ**
	+ Believers are united to Christ as members to the head
	+ This union is impossible to break, just like a bone in Christ's natural body
	+ Christ and his members make one body, and it is impossible for any part of Christ to perish

• **Argument 5: Nature of a Purchase**
	+ Christ died to purchase believers as a people to himself forever
	+ Would Christ have shed his blood for a temporary faith?
	+ Christ will not lose his purchase, and believers will persevere in faith

• **Argument 6: Victory over the World**
	+ A believer who overcomes the world perseveres in faith
	+ A believer may be foiled in a single battle, but ultimately wins the victory
	+ If a saint is crowned victorious, they must needs persevere

• **Arminian Objection 1: Purpose of Admonitions**
	+ Arminians argue that if believers will certainly persevere, admonitions in Scripture are superfluous
	+ Admonitions are necessary to caution believers against carelessness and to encourage diligence in working out their salvation

• **Arminian Objection 2: Nature of Faith**
	+ Arminians argue that faith is a work that can be lost
	+ Augustine argues that faith is a gift from God, and therefore cannot be lost

• **Arminian Objection 3: Possibility of Falling Away**
	+ Arminians argue that it is possible for believers to fall away
	+ Augustine argues that this is impossible, as believers are united to Christ and cannot be broken off

• **Arminian Objection 4: Role of Human Effort**
	+ Arminians argue that human effort is necessary for perseverance
	+ Augustine argues that perseverance is a gift from God, and therefore human effort is not necessary

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Objection 1: Abiding in Christ**
	+ Christ exhorts his disciples to abide in him, not to question their ability to do so
	+ The exhortation is meant to awaken diligence and prayer, not to imply doubt

• **Objection 2: Falling away**
	+ The passage in Hebrews 6:4-6 is often misinterpreted to imply that true believers can fall away
	+ However, the apostle is actually describing hypocrites who may have had a superficial experience of faith

• **Characteristics of hypocrites**
	+ They may have had great illuminations, but still fall away (e.g. Judas)
	+ They may have received the common gifts of the Spirit, but not the special grace
	+ They may have tasted the sweetness of religion, but not been nourished by it
	+ They may have had a superficial experience of the powers of the world to come

• **Distinguishing true believers**
	+ The passage in Hebrews 6:4-6 does not speak of true believers, but rather of hypocrites
	+ The apostle's tone is one of persuasion, indicating that he believes the true believers will persevere

• **The excellence of grace**
	+ Grace is eternal and perseveres, unlike other things that are fleeting
	+ It is a substance that lasts as long as the soul and heaven last

• **Everlasting love and gratitude**
	+ The fixedness of God's love to us should excite everlasting love and gratitude
	+ God's love is perpetual and carried on to our salvation

• **Perseverance in holiness**
	+ Saints persevere in holiness solely due to the power of God
	+ We are kept by God's power, like a garrison, and it is a wonder that any Christian perseveres

• **Corruption within**
	+ Corruption within us makes it a wonder that any Christian perseveres
	+ The tares of corruption are mingled with the wheat of true faith

• **The power of God**
	+ God's power is the reason why saints persevere in holiness
	+ We are kept by God's power, and it is a wonder that any Christian perseveres

• **The importance of instruction**
	+ The passage is meant to instruct and encourage true believers
	+ It highlights the excellence of grace and the power of God to keep us.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Power of God's Grace**
	+ Grace is habitually predominant despite the presence of sin
	+ It's a wonder that sin doesn't destroy grace
	+ God's power keeps us from being destroyed by sin

	+ We are kept by the power of God, not by our own strength

	+ We are kept by God's power, not by our own wealth or status

	+ The doctrine of perseverance is a magic stone that keeps our spirits up
	+ It's a sovereign cordial that comforts us in times of trouble

	+ A believer may fall from some degrees of grace, but not from the state of grace
	+ Once in Christ, forever in Christ
	+ Our heavenly inheritance cannot be wholly alienated from us

• **The Arminian Doctrine of Falling from Grace**
	+ This doctrine is despairing and cuts the sinews of a Christian's endeavor
	+ It's like boring a hole in a vessel, making all the wine of joy run out
	+ The apostle's words about the seed of God remaining in us are contradicted by this doctrine

• **Comfort in Times of Trouble**
	+ We don't need to fear finally falling away from God
	+ Sin and temptation cannot hinder our perseverance
	+ God's power keeps us safe from these dangers

• **Sin and Temptation Cannot Hinder Perseverance**
	+ Sin humbles us, but doesn't damn us
	+ Temptation is a medicine for security, and the more Satan tempts, the more we pray
	+ Nothing can break off a believer from Christ or hinder his perseverance

• **Comfort in the Loss of Worldly Comforts**
	+ While our goods may be taken away, our grace cannot
	+ We have chosen the better part, which cannot be taken from us

• **Comfort in the Hour of Death**
	+ When all things fail, and friends take their farewell of us, yet still grace remains
	+ Death may separate all things from us, except for grace

• **Believers enter glory immediately after death**

	+ The soul does not sleep in the body for a time (2 Cor 5:8)

	+ Believers will see God face to face
	+ They will love God and lie forever in the bosom of divine love
	+ They will meet their godly relations in heaven

	+ It takes believers from among fiery serpents and places them among angels
	+ It clothes them with a robe of immortality
	+ It is a transition from a life of suffering to a life of glory

• **Saints should contemplate heavenly glory**
	+ They should spend time thinking about the glorious benefits they will have in heaven
	+ This can help them begin the life of angels while still on earth
	+ It can bring them comfort and joy in their daily lives

	+ Saints may be struggling with physical or financial needs
	+ But they can take comfort in the promise of a wealthy inheritance in heaven
	+ They can look forward to a kingdom and a life of glory

	+ We do not yet know what we will be in heaven (1 John 3:2)
	+ But we can take comfort in the promise of a glorious inheritance
	+ We can look forward to being emparadised with glory

	+ Believers will take more delight in contemplating the glory of heaven than in earthly possessions
	+ They will be able to see the glory of heaven face to face
	+ They will be able to experience the joy and love of God in a way that is not possible on earth

• **Faith can help us see the glory of heaven**
	+ We can send forth faith as a spy to view the glory of heaven
	+ This can bring us joy and comfort in our daily lives
	+ It can help us to look forward to the day when we will see God face to face

• **The promise of heaven is a motivation to live a holy life**
	+ Believers should strive to live a life that is pleasing to God
	+ They should seek to be holy and blameless in their conduct
	+ They should look forward to the day when they will be with God in heaven

• **The promise of heaven is a source of comfort in times of sorrow**
	+ Believers may be experiencing sorrow or grief in their lives
	+ But they can take comfort in the promise of a glorious inheritance in heaven
	+ They can look forward to a life of joy and love with God in heaven.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

* **The Reward of Suffering**
	+ Suffering is a part of being a true saint
	+ It may make us go through our sufferings with cheerfulness
	+ There are great things laid up in store for us
	+ We will drink of the fruit of the vine in the kingdom of heaven
* **The Resurrection**
	+ The time is coming when all the dead will hear the voice of God's Son and rise again
	+ Those who have done good will rise to eternal life, and those who have continued in evil will rise to judgment
* **Benefits of Believers at Resurrection**
	+ Believers will be openly acknowledged and acquitted in the day of judgment
	+ They will be made perfectly blessed in the full enjoyment of God to all eternity
* **The Body of Believers**
	+ The bodies of believers will be raised up to glory
	+ The doctrine of the resurrection is a fundamental article of our faith
* **Arguments for the Resurrection**
	+ **Scripture**: The Bible promises that God will raise us up at the last day
	+ **Christ's Resurrection**: Christ's rising from the dead is a pledge of our resurrection
	+ **God's Justice**: God will reward the bodies of the saints as well as their souls
	+ **Desire for Reunion**: The soul has a desire to be reunited with the body
* **Objections to the Resurrection**
	+ How can the body, which is consumed to ashes, rise again?
	+ It may seem to oppose reason, but it transcends it
* **The Nature of the Resurrection**
	+ The body will rise again, not in a new body, but in its own body
	+ This is not a creation, but a resurrection
* **The Importance of the Resurrection**
	+ A believer would not be completely happy if their body did not rise again
	+ The soul can subsist without the body, but it is not fully happy until it is clothed with the body

Here are 10 concise bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Resurrection in Nature**
	+ The corn seed dies before it grows into a plant
	+ Winter brings death to fruits, but spring brings resurrection
	+ Noah's olive tree sprouted after the flood, symbolizing resurrection

• **God's Power to Raise the Dead**
	+ God can raise the body from the grave more easily than waking a person from sleep
	+ God can distinguish the dust of one body from another

• **Separation of Bodies at Resurrection**
	+ The chemist can extract one metal from another, and God can do the same with bodies
	+ God can reinvest every soul with its own body

• **Resurrection of the Wicked and the Righteous**
	+ All who are in the grave will hear Christ's voice and come forth
	+ There will be a resurrection of both the just and unjust

• **Differences in Resurrection**
	+ The bodies of the wicked will be raised with ignominy and loathsomeness
	+ The bodies of the saints will be raised with honor and glory

• **Triumph and Trembling at Resurrection**
	+ The bodies of believers will rise with triumph and joy
	+ The bodies of the wicked will rise with trembling and doom

• **Importance of Believing in Resurrection**
	+ Without believing in resurrection, true religion falls to the ground
	+ If the dead do not rise, then Christ is not risen, and faith is vain

• **Job's Comfort and the Reuniting of Body and Soul**
	+ Job's comfort was that his body would rise again and see God
	+ The body is sensible of joy, and we will not be in all our glory until our bodies are reunited with our souls

• **The Reuniting of Body and Soul at Resurrection**
	+ The body and soul will greet each other with joy and welcome
	+ The soul will be eternally married to the body, which will reign with it in glory

• **The Resurrection as a Cordial for Christians Dying**
	+ The resurrection of the body is a comfort for Christians dying
	+ The body, though it drops into the sepulcher, will revive and be reunited with the soul.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Resurrection**
	+ The grave is a temporary resting place, not a final destination
	+ Our bodies will be awakened by the trumpet of the archangel
	+ We will be restored to our former beauty and glory

• **The First Resurrection**
	+ It refers to rising by repentance out of the grave of sin
	+ Those who have a part in the first resurrection will be raised to glory
	+ Regeneration is necessary for a glorious resurrection

• **Preparing for the Resurrection**
	+ Keep your bodies pure and unspotted from sin
	+ Avoid sinful behaviors such as drunkenness, unchastity, and theft
	+ Your bodies are the members of Christ, and you should glorify God with them

• **Comfort in Death**
	+ God will not leave his people in the grave
	+ He will watch over our dead bodies and take care of our ashes
	+ Our souls will be in paradise while our bodies are in the grave

• **The State of the Soul in Death**
	+ The soul does not sleep in the body, but returns to God
	+ The soul immediately partakes of the joys of paradise
	+ The soul is crowned and triumphant while the body is sleeping or buried

• **The Return of the Soul**
	+ The soul is sent before into heaven to taste of the fruit of the holy land
	+ The soul returns to rest when the body returns to dust

• **The Power of God**
	+ God will bring us out of the grave when his time comes
	+ The graves will deliver up their dead at God's command
	+ God will surely bring us up again, just as he promised Jacob

• **The Glory of the Resurrection**
	+ The bodies of the saints will be made illustrious and glorious
	+ They will be lovely and beautiful when they arise
	+ They will shine as stars in the kingdom of heaven

• **The Importance of Faith**
	+ We must have faith in God's promise of the resurrection
	+ We must trust in God's power to bring us out of the grave
	+ We must have confidence in God's goodness and love

• **The Hope of the Resurrection**
	+ We have hope in the resurrection, not just for ourselves, but for our loved ones who have died
	+ We can trust that God will bring them out of the grave and restore them to glory
	+ We can have confidence in God's promise to raise us up on the last day.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Resurrection of the Body**
	+ The body of a saint in this life may be deformed, but it will be attractive and beautiful in the resurrection.
	+ The beauty of the resurrected body consists of:
		- Perfection of parts (full proportion of all members)
		- Splendor (graceful majesty)

• **Perfection of Parts**
	+ The resurrected body will have all its parts restored, including those that were lost or damaged in this life.
	+ This is why the resurrection is called the time of restoring all things (Acts 3:21).

• **Splendor**
	+ The bodies of the saints will have a gracious and majestic appearance.
	+ They will be made like Christ's glorious body (Phil 3:21).

• **Freedom from Necessities of Nature**
	+ The resurrected bodies will be free from hunger and thirst.
	+ They will be filled with God's glory and upheld without food.

• **Swiftness and Nimbleness**
	+ The resurrected bodies will be swift and nimble, like the body of Elijah.
	+ They will be able to ascend and move instantly, like the angels.

• **Firmness and Strength**
	+ The resurrected bodies will be firm and strong, with no weariness or faintness.
	+ They will be like a rock, not a weak reed.

• **Immortality**
	+ The resurrected bodies will be immortal, with no possibility of death.
	+ They will run parallel with eternity and be free from the bill of mortality.

• **The Day of Judgment**
	+ There will be a day of judgment, where all people will appear before Christ's tribunal.
	+ This is a grand appearance, where even the greatest people will tremble before the Lamb's throne.

• **The Testimony of Scripture**
	+ The Bible testifies to the existence of a day of judgment.
	+ Scripture says that God will bring every work into judgment, with every secret thing.

• **The Petty Sessions of Conscience**
	+ The conscience is a private session that arraigns and condemns us for our evil deeds.
	+ This is a certain forerunner of the general day of judgment.

• **The Purpose of the Day of Judgment**
	+ The day of judgment is necessary to acquit the righteous and condemn the wicked.
	+ It is a time of reckoning, where God will judge all people according to their works.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Who will be the judge?**
	+ The Lord Jesus Christ
	+ As stated in John 5:22, "The Father has committed all judgment to the Son"
	+ Christ's role as judge is a great honor, as he was himself judged and is now fit to judge

• **Why is Christ fit to be judge?**
	+ He partakes of both the manhood and Godhead
	+ As man, he can be visibly seen by all
	+ As God, he has infinite knowledge and power to understand and execute judgment

• **What are the qualifications of Christ as judge?**
	+ He has seven eyes to denote his wisdom (Zech 3:9)
	+ He has an iron rod to denote his power (Psalm 2:9)
	+ He is wise and cannot be deceived
	+ He is strong and cannot be resisted

• **When will the time of judgment be?**
	+ The time of the general judgment is a secret kept from the angels
	+ It cannot be far off, as iniquity is abounding in this age
	+ The elect will be converted before Christ comes to judgment

• **What will be the manner of trial?**
	+ The citing of men to the court, including the dead
	+ The citing will be by the sound of a trumpet (1 Thess 4:16)
	+ The trumpet will raise men from their graves (Matt 24:31)

• **What will happen when Christ approaches the tribunal?**
	+ It will be terrible to the wicked
	+ They will tremble at the sight of Christ, who will be described as sitting in judgment with a fiery stream issuing from him (Dan 7:10)

• **What will happen when Christ approaches the bench of judicature?**
	+ It will be comfortable to the righteous
	+ Christ will come in splendor and great glory, with the holy angels (Mark 8:38)

• **What is the significance of the trumpet sound?**
	+ It will be a loud sound that will raise men from their graves
	+ Those who will not hear the trumpet of the gospel will hear the trumpet of the archangel sounding, "arise, and be judged!"

• **What will be the reaction of sinners when they see Christ come to judgment?**
	+ They will be troubled at his presence
	+ They will be struck with horror and amazement at the sight of their judge

• **What will be the reaction of the righteous when they see Christ come to judgment?**
	+ They will be comforted by Christ's presence
	+ They will be comforted by Christ's glory and the presence of the holy angels

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Day of Judgment**
	+ A day when angels and Christ will appear in splendor
	+ A day of joy and happiness for the righteous
	+ A day of condemnation for the wicked

• **The Trial of the Wicked**
	+ The books of conscience and God's remembrance will be opened
	+ The wicked will be convicted of their sins and speechless
	+ They will be sentenced to "everlasting fire, prepared for the devil and his angels"

• **The Sentence of the Wicked**
	+ The sentence is irreversible and just
	+ The wicked will cry "Guilty!" and acknowledge their sin

• **The Trial of the Righteous**
	+ The day of judgment will be a day of jubilee for the righteous
	+ Christ will own them by name and acknowledge them as his favorites

• **Christ's Plea for the Saints**
	+ Christ will plead his own blood for the saints
	+ He will vindicate them from all unjust censures and clear their innocence

• **Christ's Absolution of the Elect**
	+ Christ will say "I find no fault in them" and pronounce them righteous
	+ They will be called "blessed" and inherit the kingdom

• **The Good Deeds of the Saints**
	+ Christ will mention before men and angels all the good deeds the saints have done
	+ He will say "For I was hungry, and you fed me" and other similar phrases

• **The Joy of the Saints**
	+ The saints will be filled with ravishing joy at the hearing of the sentence
	+ The word "Come, you blessed!" will be music to their ears

• **The Role of Christ as Judge**
	+ Christ will be both judge and advocate for the saints
	+ He will plead their case and clear their innocence

• **The Comfort of the Saints**
	+ The saints can comfort themselves with the day of judgment
	+ They will know that Christ will vindicate them and pronounce them righteous

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **The Day of Judgment is a Time of Reward**
	+ Christ will take notice of good works and reward them
	+ He will proclaim the praises of those who have served him faithfully

• **The Saints Will Judge the World**
	+ Christ will call his saints to sit with him in judgment
	+ They will applaud Christ's righteous sentence on the wicked

• **The Saints Will Be Fully Crowned**
	+ They will enjoy God's presence forever
	+ They will experience fullness of joy and be with the Lord eternally

• **The Wicked Will Face Judgment**
	+ They will not stand in judgment, but will come to judgment
	+ They will not be able to look their judge in the face

• **The Day of Judgment is Comfort for the Weak**
	+ A Christian's imperfect grace will be accepted by Christ
	+ True gold, even in small amounts, will be valued by Christ

• **Christ Will Righteous Judgment**
	+ He will judge things over again and give a righteous sentence
	+ Those who have been wronged will be restored a thousandfold

• **Meditation on the Day of Judgment**
	+ Should make us sincere and labor to approve our hearts to God
	+ Should keep us from judging our brethren

• **Living for the Day of Judgment**
	+ Should motivate us to live a life that will result in acquittal
	+ Should lead us to labor to get into Christ through faith

• **Faith is the Key to Acquittal**
	+ Faith implants us into Christ and engarrisons us in him
	+ There is no condemnation for those who are in Christ

• **The Privileges of the Saints**
	+ They will be crowned with the glorious privileges of heaven
	+ They will experience the joys of heaven without intermission or expiration

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• **Be Humble**
	+ Recognize your own nothingness and limitations
	+ Cover your abilities with humility, like Moses covering his face
	+ A humble person will be acquitted at the day of judgment

• **Keep a Clear Conscience**
	+ Avoid loading yourself with guilt and sin
	+ Be careful of the first and second tables of the law (be holy and just)
	+ Keep your conscience clear and free from sin

• **Trade with Your Talents for God's Glory**
	+ Use your abilities and resources to honor God
	+ Trade with your talents to make them more valuable
	+ Be a good and faithful servant to your Lord

• **Get a Sincere Love for the Saints**
	+ Love is the truest touchstone of sincerity
	+ Delight in the graces and image of God in others
	+ Bear with the infirmities of others and reverence their graces

• **Be Acquitted at the Day of Judgment**
	+ A humble person will be acquitted
	+ A person with a clear conscience will be acquitted
	+ A person who trades with their talents for God's glory will be acquitted

• **The Voice of Conscience is the Voice of God**
	+ If conscience acquits us, God will acquit us
	+ If our heart condemns us not, we have confidence toward God

• **Love is a Blessed Evidence**
	+ A sincere love for the saints is a sign of God's spirit in a person
	+ We know we have passed from death to life because we love the brethren

• **Be Holy and Just**
	+ Be careful of the first and second tables of the law
	+ Keep your heart and hands free from sin and false aims

• **Trade with Your Talents Wisely**
	+ Use your abilities and resources to make them more valuable
	+ Be a good and faithful servant to your Lord

• **Stand with Boldness at the Day of Judgment**
	+ Keep a good conscience to stand with boldness at the day of judgment
	+ Be acquitted and confident in the face of judgment
